The Uniformity Theorem for p-adic Splitting Densities, Mapped

The Lean 4 formalization as an expandable graph — every node carries the formal statement and its mathematical translation. Companion to the expository account. Built July 2026.

How to read this page. The page has four levels. The story below reads on its own. Each numbered stage expands to a summary of one step of the proof. Inside a stage, each module group expands to its role and its main results. Each result expands to a plain-mathematics translation, and one further click shows the exact Lean statement. Status badges say what kind of certificate each node carries; the legend is at the bottom, and the honest boundary of the whole development is in the amber panel.

The theorem

The factorization-density theorem for p-adic polynomials — conjectured by Bhargava, Cremona, Fisher, and Gajović; tame case proved by G–Wei–Yin (arXiv:2212.00294), wild case OPEN — approached by an independent ALGORITHMIC route and machine-checked in Lean. Certified unconditionally: for degree two, each of the three splitting types has one fixed rational function giving its density at every prime, including the wild prime 2. Certified conditionally for every fixed degree: at any prime whose counting data passes an explicit two-clause regularity gate, the density of every splitting type equals one fixed rational function of the residue-field size, the candidates summing to 1 identically. The amber panel below is the exact certification boundary.

The story

Take a monic polynomial of some fixed degree with coefficients in the p-adic integers $\mathbb{Z}_p$, each coefficient drawn digit-by-digit uniformly at random — the finite-depth truncations of this recipe are mutually consistent, and together they define the normalized Haar measure, the only probability model used anywhere on this page. Over the p-adic field the polynomial factors into irreducible pieces, and each piece is measured by two whole numbers whose product is its degree: its ramification index (how much finer the ladder of p-adic sizes becomes in the extension its root generates) and its residue degree (how much larger the finite field of remainders becomes). The multiset of these pairs is the polynomial's splitting type; for quadratics the three types are split, inert, and ramified. Each type is a measurable event, so it has a probability at each prime — its density — and the question is how that density varies with the prime.

A prime exceeding the degree is called tame here: it is then coprime to every ramification index that can occur at that degree. At tame primes the density calculus is classical: Hensel's lemma lifts factorizations of the reduction whenever the reduction has no repeated factors, counting over the residue field gives polynomial formulas, and tame ramification is classified by explicit weights, so each density is the value of a fixed rational function of the residue-field size. At a wild prime — one at most the degree — the polynomials that matter are exactly those whose reductions degenerate, Hensel separation fails there, and the geometric route behind the tame theorem also breaks (its symmetric-group-equivariant resolution needs a linearly reductive group, which fails in residue characteristic up to the degree). What remains is digit-by-digit analysis in which carries — overflow between digit positions — decide factorizations at depths that vary from polynomial to polynomial. From low-degree computations, Bhargava, Cremona, Fisher, and Gajović conjectured that the tame formulas persist at wild primes. G, Wei, and Yin (arXiv:2212.00294) proved the tame case, functional equation included, via Poincaré duality, and proposed a geometric route to the wild case; that construction does not hold at wild primes as stated, and the wild case is open. The development mapped here is an independent, algorithmic route toward it, machine-checked from the outset.

For degree two, the development proves the connection unconditionally: the ramified type has density $1/(q+1)$ and the split and inert types each $q/(2q+2)$, where $q$ is the residue-field size, at every prime — so at the wild prime 2 all three equal $1/3$. The same rational functions the tame computation produces at odd primes give the wild value; nothing new happens at 2 except the length of the road. This quadratic theorem, including the vanishing of the undecided remainder, carries a certificate resting only on Lean's three built-in logical axioms plus its standard mathematical library.

For higher degrees the development builds the same mechanism in full and certifies it conditionally. The Montes–Okutsu classification algorithm — read a Newton polygon, factor a residual polynomial over a growing finite field, recenter, repeat — organizes all polynomials into a tree, and the set of polynomials at any node is proved to be an explicit finite union of digit cylinders, so its measure is readable off pinned digits. Two finiteness results control the tree. First, only a bounded packet of a node's data (slope data, residual class, a bounded carry window) influences its subtree: the packet is the node's pattern (the development says species), the number of possible patterns is proved finite with an explicit bound at every degree, and at degree three the catalogue is machine-enumerated: exactly 53. Second, the carry interactions that make wild primes hard are computed once, inside a fixed quotient ring $\mathbb{F}[u]/(u^e - \bar z)$ — the carry algebra — rather than re-derived prime by prime. The transitions out of a node depend only on its pattern, through rational expressions in the residue-field size.

Counting then reduces to linear algebra: one linear relation per pattern, a Neumann series over depths, and a symbolic matrix inversion (determinant verified nonzero at the evaluation points used) produce one candidate rational function per splitting type, with the candidates summing to 1 identically; at degree three the full tables re-derived inside the development match the independently recorded values exactly, wild primes included. The capstone theorem then proves the squeeze: from a per-prime instance bundle — level-by-level decided counts, an error envelope that vanishes with depth, regularity data, and a bridge to genuine coefficient classes — the density of each type exists at every regular prime, equals the candidate value, and does so in three provably equivalent readings. Constructing those instance bundles from the classifier at every degree beyond the certified cases is the main remaining work, and the amber panel states exactly what stands proved, what is verified computationally, and what is open.

The formalization's contribution is a typed boundary: every assumption of the capstone is a named hypothesis in its formal statement, the proof assistant prints each theorem's axiom footprint (for the two capstones: exactly Lean's three built-in axioms), and each open item is a typed statement checkable by the same kernel the day a proof is supplied. Statements were accepted only after adversarial review in fresh contexts, with refuted formulations replaced and recorded.

The capstone, exactly

Fix a degree at least two. The theorem's premises are, in order: carrier structures packaging the counting apparatus (the parameters named C and KC in the formal text) and a kernel package (K7); a solve output (S) carrying one candidate rational function per splitting type; a ledger of typed statements over the proved corpora (KT); thirteen named propositional slots with their witnesses — these are the placeholder hypotheses discussed in the amber panel: typed positions whose intended mathematical content is not yet formulated, currently satisfiable trivially; and, THE LOAD-BEARING PREMISE, an instance bundle for every prime, packaging that prime's counting data (level-by-level decided counts, classifier-keyed and true-type density masses, an undecided-mass envelope), its regularity data, and its bridge to p-adic coefficient classes. Constructing these instance bundles from the classifier, at every degree beyond the certified cases, is the main open work of the development — this premise is where the remaining mathematics lives. The conclusion: there is a family assigning to each splitting type a rational function — exactly the solve's candidates — summing to 1 identically, such that at every prime whose instance passes the two-clause regularity gate: the decided count at every reading depth is trapped in the displayed two-sided bracket; the classifier-keyed density mass converges to the candidate value; the true-type density mass (connected through the verdict-soundness hypothesis of the ledger) converges to the same value; the density read directly from p-adic coefficient classes (invariant pairs read as ramification index and residue degree) converges to the same value; and the undecided envelope converges to zero. At a prime failing the gate, the theorem asserts nothing.

The Lean statement of Theorem U 
theorem theoremU (n : ℕ) (hn : 2 ≤ n) (C : UCarriers n) (KC : KernelCarriers n C)
    (K7 : Cl7Kernel n KC) (S : SolveData n)
    (KT : UpstreamTyped n KC)
    (rel1 rel2a rel2b rel2d rel2e rel3 : Prop)
    (rs0Lump trackRule dnLattice : Prop)
    (m1m5Echo x1aDict m4bConst jcInvHist : Prop)
    (hrel1 : rel1) (hrel2a : rel2a) (hrel2b : rel2b) (hrel2d : rel2d)
    (hrel2e : rel2e) (hrel3 : rel3)
    (hrs0 : rs0Lump) (htrk : trackRule) (hdn : dnLattice)
    (hm15 : m1m5Echo) (hx1a : x1aDict) (hm4b : m4bConst) (hjc : jcInvHist)
    (inst : ∀ (p : ℕ) (hp : p.Prime), UInstance n C KC K7 S p hp) :
    ∃ R : SplittingType n → RatFunc ℚ,
      R = S.R ∧
      (∑ σ, R σ = 1) ∧
      ∀ (p : ℕ) (hp : p.Prime), RegP (inst p hp).D →
        -- (U-n): the exported two-sided bracket
        (∀ (σ : SplittingType n) (N : ℕ),
          (evalℝ ⟨R⟩ σ p - (inst p hp).X.env N) * (p : ℝ) ^ (n * N)
              ≤ ((inst p hp).X.decided σ N : ℝ) ∧
            ((inst p hp).X.decided σ N : ℝ)
              ≤ evalℝ ⟨R⟩ σ p * (p : ℝ) ^ (n * N))
        -- (i) the classifier-keyed density
        ∧ (∀ σ : SplittingType n,
          Tendsto ((inst p hp).X.dmass σ) atTop (𝓝 (evalℝ ⟨R⟩ σ p)))
        -- (ii) the true-type density (via verdict soundness)
        ∧ (∀ σ : SplittingType n,
          Tendsto ((inst p hp).X.trueDmass σ) atTop (𝓝 (evalℝ ⟨R⟩ σ p)))
        -- (ii-ℤ_p) the ℤ_p-read density (coefficient classes; (ramIdx, resDeg) pairs)
        ∧ (∀ σ : SplittingType n,
          Tendsto (@ZpBridge.zpDmass n p ⟨hp⟩ _ (inst p hp).bridge σ) atTop
            (𝓝 (evalℝ ⟨R⟩ σ p)))
        -- (iii) the undecided complement has mass 0
        ∧ Tendsto (inst p hp).X.env atTop (𝓝 0)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesU/U10_theoremU.lean — axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound (Lean core only)

The proof, stage by stage

1 The Okutsu–Montes classifier tree

The classification algorithm — Newton polygon, residual polynomial over a growing finite field, recenter, repeat — is formalized in full: polygons, residual polynomials, developments, and the tree of types. This subsystem carries the two unconditional density capstones: the complete degree-2 uniformity theorem at every prime (wild 2 included, undecided remainder proved vanishing), and an order-0 density theorem at general degree covering the stratum decided at the first level. Everything deeper at general degree feeds the conditional architecture of the later stages.

$$R_{\mathrm{ram}}(q)=\frac{1}{q+1},\qquad R_{\mathrm{inert}}(q)=R_{\mathrm{split}}(q)=\frac{q}{2q+2}\qquad(\deg = 2,\ \text{every prime, proved})$$
The classifier engine (OM) 2 cited axiom · 7 definition · 1 open · 5 proved · 5 conditional
Formalizes the Montes classifier: polygons, counting, wild resummation, guarded faithfulness

This corpus is the computational core of the formalization: a Lean model of the Okutsu-Montes higher-order Newton-polygon algorithm as a total function on monic polynomials known modulo p^N, the object that computes p-adic factorization behavior even at wildly ramified primes. It supplies the counting theory that turns the classifier's per-shape strata into a single rational function of q — with an explicit split between guarded abstract results and instantiated ones: the assembly and wild self-loop resummation theorems are proved as implications from per-shape counting-limit hypotheses (discharged for the tame/separable shapes, open for the wild self-loop shapes), while the general-p exhaustion covering and the degree-2 instantiations (including the full n = 2, p = 2 density tie with proved exhaustion) are instantiated theorems. A faithfulness layer ties classifier fibers to genuine Q_p-factorizations; it rests on exactly two cited Guardia-Montes-Nart axioms, each scoped to the shape menus, guarded by axiom-free anchor theorems and machine-checked mutation refutations — the density theorems themselves use no axioms beyond Lean's core. The real-instance and uniformity capstones (separate corpora) consume its classifier, shape menus, coverings, and faithfulness theorems.

Builds on: Interface, MontesAxiom, MontesV2, CountingModel, RatFn, L3, L4, L5fix

definition The lower Newton polygon as a computable convex hull

The polygon is built as a max of supporting chords (not mathlib's set-theoretic convexHull) so vertices, sides and slopes are computable data; the file records a repair: an earlier min-of-lines draft was concave (an infimum of affine maps), refuted by the three-dot example {(0,3),(1,0),(2,3)}, and was replaced by the max-over-valid-supporting-lines form whose convexity is proved.

For a nonempty finite set $S \subset \mathbb{N}^2$ of dots $(i,v)$ — coefficient index $i$ plotted against $p$-adic valuation $v$ — the lower Newton polygon is the function $\mathrm{np}_S : \mathbb{Q} \to \mathbb{Q}$, $\mathrm{np}_S(x) = \max_{\ell}\, \ell(x)$: the maximum over the VALID candidate lines. Candidates are indexed by ordered pairs of dots of $S$ — the secant line through the two dots when their abscissae differ, and, by convention, the horizontal line through the dot for a repeated pair (a slope-0 junk value is also used when two distinct dots share an abscissa); the diagonal pairs make the family nonempty even for a singleton $S$ and anchor the value $v$ at $x = i$. A candidate is valid when it lies weakly below every dot of $S$; validity is nonempty (the horizontal line through a minimum-height dot qualifies), so the maximum is well-defined for every nonempty $S$. This is the lower convex hull built directly as data: it is convex, lies weakly below every dot, and its breakpoints (vertices) and segments (sides, with rational slopes and lattice endpoints) are extracted as finite lists.

The Lean statement 
variable (S : Finset (ℕ × ℕ))

noncomputable def npHeight (hS : S.Nonempty) (x : ℚ) : ℚ :=
  (validLines S).sup' (validLines_nonempty S hS) (fun PR => pairLine PR.1 PR.2 x)

lean/LeanUrat/OM/NewtonPolygon.lean — definition

definition The order-r type: the data one node of the Montes tree carries

The size law d_r ≤ cs replaced an earlier strict law that was false at the generic root and had silently made the first density instance vacuous (the since-quarantined predecessor chain) — the repair is documented in the field's docstring; the law is carried as a proof field the recursion maintains (cited Guardia-Montes-Nart content carried as a hypothesis — a deliberate footprint choice, not a free axiom).

An order-$r$ OM type bundles a residue tower of finite fields $F_0 \subseteq F_r$ with residue-extension degree $\delta = [F_r : F_0]$, the order-$r$ lattice Newton polygon $N_r$, the residual polynomial $R_r \in F_r[Y]$ with residual degree $d_r = \deg R_r$, the key-polynomial degree $m_r$, and a cluster size $\mathrm{cs}$, subject to the ties $\delta = [F_r:F_0]$, $d_r = \deg R_r$, and the size law $d_r \le \mathrm{cs}$.

The Lean statement 
structure OMType where
  order : ℕ
  F0 : Type
  Fr : Type
  [field_F0 : Field F0]
  [finite_F0 : Finite F0]
  [field_Fr : Field Fr]
  [finite_Fr : Finite Fr]
  [alg : Algebra F0 Fr]
  δ : ℕ
  Nr : L4.LatticePolygon
  Rr : Fr[X]
  dr : ℕ
  keyDeg : ℕ
  cs : ℕ
  hResidueDeg : δ = Module.finrank F0 Fr
  hResidualDeg : dr = Rr.natDegree
  hDrLeCs : dr ≤ cs

lean/LeanUrat/OM/OMType.lean — structure

definition The digit expansion: a bijection of finite coefficient boxes

The engine's basic change of coordinates — every deeper OM order reads digits of this expansion; the two-sided-inverse proofs are machine-checked core-only, and the file records a blueprint erratum (the bijection is false at N = 0, so 0 < N is required).

Over $R = \mathbb{Z}/p^N$ with $N \ge 1$, fix a monic $\varphi \in R[X]$ of degree $m$. Every monic $g$ of degree $km$ has a unique $\varphi$-adic expansion $g = \varphi^k + \sum_{j<k} b_j\,\varphi^j$ with digit polynomials $\deg b_j < m$, and $g \mapsto (b_0, \dots, b_{k-1})$ is a bijection between the monic degree-$km$ box and $k$-tuples of digits; counting both sides recovers $(p^N)^{km}$.

The Lean statement 
variable (p : ℕ) [hp : Fact p.Prime]

noncomputable def developEquiv (N m k : ℕ) (hN : 0 < N) (φ : (ZMod (p ^ N))[X])
    (hφ : φ.Monic) (hm : φ.natDegree = m) (_hm0 : 0 < m) :
    QuotientBox.monicBox p N (k * m) ≃ (Fin k → Polynomial.degreeLT (ZMod (p ^ N)) m)

lean/LeanUrat/OM/Development.lean — definition

definition The truncated classifier: running Montes on a polynomial known mod p^N

THE central object: the densities of the whole project are defined by counting this function's fibers, and the faithfulness layer (cited axioms + axiom-free anchors) is exactly what ties its output to genuine Q_p factorizations.

For a monic degree-$n$ polynomial $f$ over $\mathbb{Z}/p^N$, the classifier runs the OM iteration — order-0 Newton polygon of $f$, residual factorization over the residue field, one child per distinct residual factor, descent (via the $\varphi$-adic development) on repeated factors — under a well-founded degree budget, and outputs the resulting finite tree shape. It is a total function from the finite box of monic polynomials mod $p^N$ to discrete tree shapes, so its fibers can be counted exactly.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def classify (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (n : ℕ) :
    (N : ℕ) → QuotientBox.monicBox p N n → ClusterShape :=
  fun N f => classifyAux p N n n f (rootCtx p N f) (clusterSize_rootCtx p N f)

lean/LeanUrat/OM/Classifier.lean — definition

definition The finite, prime-independent menu of decided shapes

The interface both the leaf-faithfulness axiom and the density instances key on; each menu extension re-scoped the axiom, with a machine-checked recovery theorem deriving every previous scope back so consumers stay byte-unchanged.

For degree $n$, depth window $D$, height window $K$, and factorization type $\sigma$, the menu is a finite set of classifier output shapes keyed to $\sigma$: the previous-wave menu (order-0 leaves, mixed-slope literals, descend chains ending in ramified/inert/two-sided-split leaves) united with the split-residual family that first becomes nonempty at $p > 2$. No prime appears in the definition.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def omMenu7 (n D K : ℕ) (σ : FactorizationType) : Finset ClusterShape :=
  ChainMenu6.omMenu6 n D K σ ∪ splitUShapes n D K σ

theorem omMenu6_subset_omMenu7 (n D K : ℕ) (σ : FactorizationType) :
    ChainMenu6.omMenu6 n D K σ ⊆ omMenu7 n D K σ

lean/LeanUrat/OM/ChainMenu7.lean — definition

definition What a genuine p-adic factorization certificate is

The genuine arithmetic vocabulary, distinct from classifier output: faithfulness theorems conclude the existence of such a certificate, while the density computations never depend on one — that separation is what keeps the density capstones' axiom footprints down to Lean's core.

A certificate for monic $f \in \mathbb{Z}_p[X]$ is a list of monic polynomials $g_1, \dots, g_k$ with $\prod_i g_i = f$, each irreducible over $\mathbb{Q}_p$, each carrying local-field data for $\mathbb{Q}_p[X]/(g_i)$. Its type is the multiset $\{(e_i, f_i)\}$ of ramification indices and residue degrees, read from the bundles via mathlib's ramification/inertia vocabulary.

The Lean statement 
structure QpFactorization (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (f : ℤ_[p][X]) where
  factors : List (ℤ_[p][X])
  prod_eq : factors.prod = f
  monic : ∀ g ∈ factors, g.Monic
  irred : ∀ g ∈ factors, Irreducible (g.map (algebraMap ℤ_[p] ℚ_[p]))
  data : ∀ g ∈ factors, LocalFactorData p g

noncomputable def qpType {f : ℤ_[p][X]} (F : QpFactorization p f) : FactorizationType :=
  ⟨(F.factors.pmap (fun g hg => efOf p (F.data g hg)) (fun _ h => h) : List (ℕ × ℕ))⟩

lean/LeanUrat/OM/QpType.lean — structure

cited axiom Cited: the theorem of the index bounds every valuation the classifier reads

Replaces a FALSE uniform version that asserted the guards for every g — refuted by g = xⁿ + pᴺ, which the bulk hypothesis now excludes; one of the false axioms the standing faithfulness audit caught, with a faithfulness entry in docs/AXIOM_FAITHFULNESS.md.

For $g$ monic of degree $n$ over $\mathbb{Z}/p^{N+1}$ in the bulk — i.e. with discriminant valuation $v_p(\mathrm{disc}\,g) < N$ — every coefficient valuation the classifier reads (on its own hull dots, and on its own sides' columns up to the residual degree) is $< N$. This is the read-set-restricted, $f$-keyed form of Guardia-Montes-Nart's theorem of the index, $\mathrm{ind}(f) \le v_p(\mathrm{disc}\,f)$ (arXiv:0807.2620, Thm 4.18/Cor 4.19); it makes the classifier's reading stable under reduction of the truncation level above the discriminant valuation.

The Lean statement 
axiom omReadValuation_lt_of_certLevel_fkeyed (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] {n : ℕ} (N : ℕ) (hN : 0 < N)
    (g : QuotientBox.monicBox p (N + 1) n)
    (hbulk : PadicLift.zmodValuation p (N + 1) (g.1.discr) < N) :
    GuardSuppR p N hN g ∧ GuardSideR p N hN g

lean/LeanUrat/OM/Classifier.lean — theorem

cited axiom Cited: menu fibers genuinely factor with the keyed type

Existence-only and menu-fiber-scoped, never universal — no uniformity, rationality, measure or density term appears in it; consumed only by the faithfulness theorems (the density capstones use no axioms beyond Lean's core), and its (e,f) payload is corroborated by axiom-free anchors plus seven recorded mutation refutations.

For every prime $p$, degree $n$, type $\sigma$, menu shape $T$ in the $\sigma$-keyed menu, and monic $f \in \mathbb{Z}_p[X]$ of degree $n$ whose truncated digit data lies in $T$'s dispatch fiber, there exists a genuine $\mathbb{Q}_p$-factorization certificate of $f$ of type exactly $\sigma$. Cited to Guardia-Montes-Nart Thm 1.15 + 1.19 + Cor 1.20 (the order-1 leaf dichotomy with explicit $(e,f)$), jointly with the standard local-field structure theory the paper presupposes (Serre, Local Fields I-II), which mathlib cannot yet supply at the pin.

The Lean statement 
axiom om_leaf_faithful (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (n N : ℕ) (hN : 0 < N)
    (σ : FactorizationType) (T : ClusterShape) (D K : ℕ)
    (hT : T ∈ ChainMenu7.omMenu7 n D K σ)
    (f : Polynomial ℤ_[p]) (hf : f.Monic) (hdeg : f.natDegree = n)
    (hfib : fiberOf7 p n N hN T f hf hdeg) :
    ∃ F : QpType.QpFactorization p f, QpType.qpType p F = σ

lean/LeanUrat/OM/OmLeafFaithful.lean — theorem

proved Axiom-free anchor: Eisenstein polynomials are totally ramified

Proved from mathlib alone (no axioms beyond Lean's core); it machine-forces the cited leaf axiom's (e,f) payload at the wild gate stratum, so the axiom's conclusion there is guarded by proof, not trusted from the citation.

For monic $g \in \mathbb{Z}_p[X]$, Eisenstein at $p$, with $\deg g > 0$: every local-factor data bundle for $g$ has ramification index $e = \deg g$ and residue degree $f = 1$ — the classical total ramification of Eisenstein polynomials, proved for the exact bundle vocabulary the faithfulness axiom concludes in.

The Lean statement 
theorem ef_of_eisenstein {g : ℤ_[p][X]} (hg : g.Monic)
    (hEis : g.IsEisensteinAt (maximalIdeal ℤ_[p])) (hdeg : 0 < g.natDegree)
    (D : QpType.LocalFactorData p g) :
    QpType.eOf p D = g.natDegree ∧ QpType.fOf p D = 1

lean/LeanUrat/OM/QpTypeEisenstein.lean — theorem

proved Refutation record: swapping the ramification data in the axiom yields contradiction

Machine-checked 2026-07-21 as a scratch compiled against the live modules, with the zero-error output and axiom census recorded verbatim in the file's evidence block (the scratch itself is not committed to the library build); one of seven recorded mutation refutations (split-type, wrong-e, inert/split swaps at successive gates) pinning the cited axiom's payload in both coordinates.

Take the leaf axiom's hypotheses verbatim but mutate its conclusion type from $\{(2,1)\}$ (ramified quadratic) to $\{(1,2)\}$ (inert quadratic). Then $\bot$ is derivable: the gate fiber is nonempty, the mutated axiom forces a singleton factorization whose factor carries $(e,f)=(1,2)$, while the axiom-free Eisenstein anchor forces $(e,f)=(2,1)$ on the same bundle.

The Lean statement 
axiom om_leaf_faithful_MUTATED_efswap (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (n N : ℕ) (hN : 0 < N)
    (σ : FactorizationType) (T : ClusterShape) (hT : T ∈ WildMenu.omMenuW n σ)
    (f : Polynomial ℤ_[p]) (hf : f.Monic) (hdeg : f.natDegree = n)
    (hfib : B.classify p n N (PadicLift.toBox p n N hN f hf hdeg) = T) :
    ∃ F : QpType.QpFactorization p f,
      QpType.qpType p F = (⟨{((1 : ℕ), (2 : ℕ))}⟩ : FactorizationType)

theorem mutation_efswap_refuted : False

lean/LeanUrat/OM/QpTypeEisenstein.lean — countermodel

open Open: vertex stability of the hull under dot changes (the one fenced incomplete proof)

The classic OM corpus's sole unproved goal, deliberately fenced (the newer development corpora carry their own fenced goals, censused separately — see the axiom-census node): its only consumer is a hull-invariance chain that is off every capstone path (the menu-checking route bypassed it), and the docstring itemizes exactly the two list/adjacency facts that remain.

If every hull vertex of $S'$ is a dot of $S$, every dot of $S$ lies weakly above $S'$'s lower hull, and every abscissa occurring in $S$ occurs in $S'$, then $S$ and $S'$ have the same Newton-polygon vertex list. A true, purely combinatorial statement about lower convex hulls of finite lattice dot sets.

The Lean statement 
theorem npVertices_stable_of_hull_preserved (S S' : Finset (ℕ × ℕ)) (hS : S.Nonempty) (hS' : S'.Nonempty)
    (hpres : ∀ P ∈ NewtonPolygon.npVertices S' hS', P ∈ S)
    (habove : ∀ P ∈ S, NewtonPolygon.npHeight S' hS' (P.1 : ℚ) ≤ (P.2 : ℚ))
    (habs : ∀ P ∈ S, ∃ Q ∈ S', Q.1 = P.1) :
    NewtonPolygon.npVertices S hS = NewtonPolygon.npVertices S' hS'

lean/LeanUrat/OM/Classifier.lean — lemma

conditional Assembly: per-shape counting limits give one rational density function

The implication itself is machine-checked (no axioms beyond Lean's core); rationality needs no per-leaf hypothesis because the engine value's rationality is proved for every shape (cluster nodes included), and a soundness gate shows the skeleton reproduces the unconditional order-0 capstone when specialized to the separable menu.

Let $M$ be any finite menu of shapes. Assume for each $T \in M$ the counting limit: the number of monic degree-$n$ polynomials mod $2^N$ classified to shape $T$, divided by $2^{nN}$, converges to the engine value $\mathrm{omCount}(T,2)$ as $N \to \infty$ (the formalization's fixed real prime is $p = 2$). Then there exist polynomials $\mathrm{num}, \mathrm{den}$ with $\mathrm{den} \ne 0$ and $\mathrm{den}(q) \ne 0$ for every integer $q > 1$, such that $\sum_{T \in M} \mathrm{omCount}(T,q) = \mathrm{num}(q)/\mathrm{den}(q)$ for all $q > 1$, and the real normalized menu-sum counts converge to $\mathrm{num}(2)/\mathrm{den}(2)$.

Hypotheses this rests on: h_node — for every menu shape T, the normalized classifier-fiber counts converge to the engine value omCount(T, 2); discharged unconditionally for the tame/separable shapes, open for the wild self-loop shapes

The Lean statement

lean/LeanUrat/OM/MontesAllOrders.lean — theorem

conditional The complete conditional density theorem: one explicit wild hypothesis

The wild hypothesis is deliberately stated at the aggregate limit level because five per-depth count-identity routes to it were refuted with exact counterexamples (the self-loop wall); everything beyond the two named inputs is unconditional composition, its footprint verified in the file's axiom census (Lean core only).

For any menu $M$ and any self-loop predicate: given (i) the per-shape counting limit for every non-self-loop shape of $M$ (supplied unconditionally by the proved tame theorems), and (ii) the aggregate self-loop counting limit — the single wild hypothesis — numerically supported by Monte-Carlo/PARI sampling, but its general statement remains a conjecture — the menu density $q \mapsto \sum_{T \in M} \mathrm{omCount}(T,q)$ is one rational function $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ with denominator nonvanishing at every $q > 1$, and the real ($p=2$) normalized counts converge to $\mathrm{num}(2)/\mathrm{den}(2)$.

Hypotheses this rests on: hTame — per-shape counting limits for the non-self-loop shapes (discharged, with no axioms beyond Lean's core, for the separable / multi-slope menus); SelfLoopHNode — the aggregate wild self-loop counting limit (MC/PARI-confirmed, open)

lean/LeanUrat/OM/ConditionalDensity.lean — theorem

conditional The tame case needs no hypothesis at all

Certifies where the wild difficulty genuinely lives: at tame primes the whole density theorem composes from unconditionally proved counting limits, and the one open hypothesis of the conditional theorem is vacuous.

If the menu contains no self-loop shape — which holds at every tame prime $p > n$, since a self-loop requires $p \mid e \le n$ — then, given the proved unconditional tame counting limits, the density equals the engine rational function with no wild hypothesis: the self-loop input is discharged vacuously. Tame $\Rightarrow$ fully unconditional.

Hypotheses this rests on: hNoSelfLoop — the menu has no self-loop shape (true at every tame prime p > n); hTame — the tame per-shape counting limits (discharged, with no axioms beyond Lean's core, for the separable order-0 shapes by a companion theorem in the same file)

The Lean statement

lean/LeanUrat/OM/ConditionalDensity.lean — theorem

definition The single wild hypothesis, and the wall that forced its shape

Five per-depth finite-level count-identity routes to this limit were refuted, each with an exact counterexample (the refutation record is kept on file; a companion module proves the tempting filtration frame counts a strictly larger residue-blind event), so the hypothesis is taken at exactly the aggregate level that numerical evidence supports and nothing stronger.

For a shape $T$, the counting limit asserts that the number of monic degree-$n$ polynomials mod $2^N$ classified to $T$, divided by $2^{nN}$, converges to $\mathrm{omCount}(T,2)$. The self-loop hypothesis asserts this limit for every self-loop shape — the wild shapes whose OM tree restarts the same cluster at ever deeper levels.

The Lean statement 
def hNodeLimit (n : ℕ) (T : ClusterShape) : Prop :=
  Filter.Tendsto
    (fun N => M8.stratumCount (M9.rawCount n) T N / (M9.realP : ℚ) ^ (n * N))
    Filter.atTop
    (nhds (omCount T M9.realP))

def SelfLoopHNode (n : ℕ) (isSelfLoop : ClusterShape → Prop) : Prop :=
  ∀ T : ClusterShape, isSelfLoop T → hNodeLimit n T

lean/LeanUrat/OM/ConditionalDensity.lean — definition

proved The wild self-loop density is a geometric fixpoint

No axioms beyond Lean's core; this is the mechanism that lets wildly ramified strata sum to a rational function of q — an infinite tower of ever-deeper restarts of the same cluster, resummed as a geometric series, tied in the same file to an actual engine value rather than a free constant.

Let $r = q^{-w(s)}$ with $w(s) = s(s+1)/2 - 1$ be the exposed per-level restart ratio of a size-$s$ self-loop cluster ($s, q \ge 2$). The closed value $b = d_0(1-r)^{-1}$ satisfies the self-consistency equation $b = d_0 + r\,b$ (the depth-0 seed plus the same cluster restarted one level deeper), is its unique solution, and equals the limit of the weighted geometric depth sums $d_0 \sum_{k<d} r^k$. At $p = q = 2$, $s = 2$, seed $d_0 = 1/2$, it lands exactly on the engine value $2/3$.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def selfLoopFixpointValue (depth0 : ℚ) (s q : ℕ) : ℚ :=
  depth0 * (MontesAxiom.countPivot s q)⁻¹

theorem selfLoopFixpoint_eq {s q : ℕ} (hq : 2 ≤ q) (hs : 2 ≤ s) (depth0 : ℚ) :
    selfLoopFixpointValue depth0 s q
      = depth0 + slExposedRatio s q * selfLoopFixpointValue depth0 s q

lean/LeanUrat/OM/SelfLoopFixpoint.lean — theorem

conditional Degree two at the wild prime, all depths: the full density tie with proved exhaustion

Footprint is Lean core only (no cited axiom on any path); the file's scope block discloses that the sigma-keying of two counting fibers is definitional on the counting side (outside the then-declared axiom scope) and that the q-family carried here is the p=2-complete tree — the general-p model with the extra split-residual family lives in the uniform model module.

Over the complete $n = 2$, $p = 2$ stratum tree (both residue points, all depths, all leaf kinds), for each quadratic type $\sigma$: (R) the closed density family is one rational function $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ with denominator nonvanishing at every $q > 1$; (V) the genuine $N \to \infty$ decided-count limit equals $\mathrm{num}(2)/\mathrm{den}(2)$, which is $1/3$ for each of the three types; (B) bracket uniqueness holds unconditionally — any value squeezed at every level between the decided count and decided-plus-undecided count IS the density, with the exhaustion (undecided mass $\to 0$) proved outright; (P) palindromy of $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$, conditional on an explicit tame functional-equation hypothesis.

Hypotheses this rests on: htameFE — a tame functional equation for the full model, gating ONLY the palindromy clause (P); clauses (R), (V), (B) including the exhaustion are unconditional (palindromy was later placed out of the project's scope entirely)

The Lean statement

        d ≤ realMW6.decidedCount σ N / (M9.realP : ℚ) ^ (2 * N)
              + realMW6.undecidedCount N / (M9.realP : ℚ) ^ (2 * N)) →
      d = realMW6.countingDensity σ

conditional Every counted stratum genuinely factors with its keyed type

lean/LeanUrat/OM/FullFaithful.lean — theorem

proved The split leaf is proved, not cited: Hensel lifting at every prime

Axiom-free at general p (root-generic Hensel); the split-residual family is the one genuinely new stratum family at p > 2 (pool of size (q-1)(q-2)/2, machine-checked empty at q = 2), and proving it outright keeps the cited axiom's instances on this family redundant by requirement.

For every prime $p$: a monic quadratic $g$ over $\mathbb{Z}_p$ whose digit data at heights $(2m, m)$ exhibits a residual pair with two distinct simple roots splits over $\mathbb{Q}_p$ into two linear factors — type $\{(1,1),(1,1)\}$ — by Hensel lifting at each simple residual root of the rescaled polynomial, followed by an explicit proved transport of the certificate back to $g$. This is the leaf-faithfulness conclusion itself, with no axiom.

lean/LeanUrat/OM/QpTypeSplitU.lean — theorem

proved Exhaustion at every prime: each cluster pair terminates or measurably stalls

The covering that powers unconditional bracket uniqueness in the all-primes uniformity capstone: the stall remainder has mass at most $(\lfloor N/2 \rfloor + 1)\,p^{-(N-1)} \to 0$; no axioms beyond Lean's core, and cross-checked digit-by-digit by an external Python census at p = 2 and p = 3.

For every prime $p$, every height window $L_0 \le L_1$, and every coefficient pair in the cluster at a residue point: either the pair realizes a terminating chain stratum within budget — a descend prefix ending in one of the four leaf kinds (ramified, inert, two-sided split, or split-residual) — or it lies in an explicitly bounded stall stratum ($p^{L_0 - 1}$ divides the constant coefficient). Proved by strong induction on the budget, with no parity case-split between $p = 2$ and odd $p$.

The Lean statement 
variable (p : ℕ) [hp : Fact p.Prime]

theorem cluster_coveringP :
    ∀ L₀ L₁ : ℕ, L₀ ≤ L₁ →
    ∀ a : ZMod (p ^ L₀) × ZMod (p ^ L₁),
      ClusterPair p L₀ L₁ a →
      (∃ ms leaf, (∀ m ∈ ms, 1 ≤ m) ∧ leafOK leaf ∧
        2 * ms.sum + leafNeed leaf ≤ L₀ ∧ ChainPair p ms leaf L₀ L₁ a)
      ∨ (∃ ms m, (∀ x ∈ ms, 1 ≤ x) ∧ 1 ≤ m ∧
          2 * ms.sum + leafNeedU m ≤ L₀ ∧ ChainPairU p ms m L₀ L₁ a)
      ∨ (∃ ms, (∀ m ∈ ms, 1 ≤ m) ∧ (ms ≠ [] → 2 * ms.sum + 2 ≤ L₀) ∧
          StallPairP p ms L₀ L₁ a)

lean/LeanUrat/OM/UniformModelN2.lean — theorem

The order-0 and degree-2 capstones (OM/RealInstance) 5 definition · 10 proved · 5 conditional
Abstract density interfaces instantiated to machine-checked uniform densities, every prime

This corpus is the assembly layer of the formalization: it fixes the abstract interfaces a density theorem is stated against (the density foundation and the abstract-coefficient Montes data), proves the two spine goal theorems (a count-native route using no axioms beyond Lean's core, and an independent measure route resting on the cited Denef-Igusa axioms), and instantiates the spine at real, machine-checked counting models: the sigma-keyed order-0 capstone at p = 2, the first genuinely wild (p divides e) densities, and the all-primes n = 2 uniformity capstone with fixed prime-free rational functions. Machine-checked gates certify exact values (1/4, 1/8, 1/(p+1), ...) and type-separation against the real instances rather than the deliberately weak interfaces. Negative records — the device-only interface witness, the empty order-0 ramified menu, the provably empty wild stratum at p = 2 — mark exactly what is not claimed; the corpus's predecessor capstone chain was found vacuous and is quarantined, and every current capstone names its remaining conditionality (tame functional equation, exhaustion) as an explicit hypothesis.

Builds on: OM, CountingModel, RatFn, L6M4, L7, PadicMeasure, MontesAxiom

definition The abstract density interface

The interface every capstone is stated against; deliberately weak by audit-driven design — statements about a free foundation must enter theorems as hypotheses, never as axioms (an earlier global axiom over a free foundation was machine-refuted). The bridge from this abstract density to a genuine density of p-adic coefficient space is proved at the real instances: lean/LeanUrat/OM/Order0RealDensity.lean (montes_order0_realDensity) identifies the instance density with the N→∞ limit of normalized level-N classifier-fiber counts — the elementary counting form of the Haar-measure density (mathlib has no p-adic Haar measure, so the identification is via exact counts; on the Moves side the analogous landing-cylinder measure theorem is lean/LeanUrat/Moves/L6_measureExact_R4.lean).

An abstract "density foundation": an assignment $\rho(n,\sigma;q)\in\mathbb{Q}$ of a density to each degree $n$, factorization type $\sigma$ (a multiset of ramification/residue pairs $(e_i,f_i)$ with $\sum e_if_i=n$), and residue cardinality $q$; per-shape cluster volumes $C_T(q)$; a finite menu of cluster-tree shapes for each $(n,\sigma)$; polynomial multiplicities $m_T$; the stratification identity $\rho(n,\sigma;q)=\sum_{T\in\mathrm{menu}(n,\sigma)} m_T(q)\,C_T(q)$ for all $q>1$ when $\deg\sigma=n$; and nonnegativity $\rho\ge 0$. It records the cited measure-foundational facts without requiring $\rho$ to be a probability.

The Lean statement 
structure DensityFoundation where
  density : (n : ℕ) → FactorizationType → (q : ℕ) → ℚ
  clusterVolume : ClusterShape → ℕ → ℚ
  shapes : (n : ℕ) → FactorizationType → Finset ClusterShape
  multiplicity : ClusterShape → Polynomial ℚ
  decomposition : ∀ (n : ℕ) (σ : FactorizationType), σ.degree = n → ∀ q : ℕ, 1 < q →
    density n σ q = ∑ T ∈ shapes n σ, (multiplicity T).eval (q : ℚ) * clusterVolume T q
  density_nonneg : ∀ n σ q, 0 ≤ density n σ q

lean/LeanUrat/Interface.lean — structure

definition The abstract Montes counting data

The second-revision spine interface, replacing an earlier interface whose hard-coded coefficient shape was numerically refuted; deliberately weak (satisfiable by degenerate instances), so non-vacuity is enforced at the real instances by the value gates, never here.

Abstract Montes data over a counting model with residue cardinality $q$ and degree $n$ (a counting model tracks decided/undecided counts of monic degree-$n$ polynomials mod $q^N$ whose normalized decided counts converge): for each type $\sigma$ a finite menu of cluster-tree shapes $T$; level-$N$ stratum counts $\#S_T^{(N)}$; the partition law that the level-$N$ decided count of $\sigma$ equals $\sum_{T\in\mathrm{menu}(\sigma)}\#S_T^{(N)}$; an abstract per-shape coefficient $C(T,q')$ that is one fixed rational function of $q'$ with denominator nonvanishing for $q'>1$; and the counting limit $\#S_T^{(N)}/q^{nN}\to C(T,q)$ as $N\to\infty$.

The Lean statement 
structure MontesDataV2 (q n : ℕ) (M : CountingModel q n) where
  shapesOf : FactorizationType → Finset ClusterShape
  stratumCount : ClusterShape → ℕ → ℚ
  partition : ∀ (σ : FactorizationType) (N : ℕ),
    M.decidedCount σ N = ∑ T ∈ shapesOf σ, stratumCount T N
  C : ClusterShape → ℕ → ℚ
  C_isRational : ∀ T : ClusterShape, IsRationalFn (fun q' => C T q')
  stratum_tendsto_C : ∀ (σ : FactorizationType), ∀ T ∈ shapesOf σ,
    Filter.Tendsto (fun N => stratumCount T N / (q : ℚ) ^ (n * N))
      Filter.atTop (nhds (C T q))

lean/LeanUrat/MontesV2.lean — structure

definition Palindromy of a rational function, semantic form

The palindromy clause of every capstone; adopted after a statement audit caught the unfaithful coefficient-reversal form.

$R=\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ satisfies the functional equation $R(1/x)=R(x)$ at every $x\neq 0$ where both sides are defined. This is the faithful, degree-robust encoding: an earlier coefficient-reversal encoding was degree-blind and wrongly rejected palindromic functions of unequal reduced degrees such as $t/(t^2+1)$.

The Lean statement 
def IsPalindromic (num den : Polynomial ℚ) : Prop :=
  ∀ x : ℚ, x ≠ 0 → den.eval x ≠ 0 → den.eval x⁻¹ ≠ 0 →
    num.eval x⁻¹ / den.eval x⁻¹ = num.eval x / den.eval x

lean/LeanUrat/Interface.lean — definition

definition The tame functional-equation hypothesis

Formerly a global AXIOM over an arbitrary foundation, machine-refuted as false-as-stated (a legal foundation with density q forces x⁻¹ = x); now a predicate consumed only as an explicit hypothesis — its faithful real-instance discharge (via the cited Del Corso–Dvornicich functional equation) awaits the still-open exhaustiveness work plus the projective bridge.

The predicate: for a foundation $F$, degree $n$, and type $\sigma$, there is a single rational function $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ representing $F$'s density at every tame prime $q>n$, and it is palindromic ($R(1/t)=R(t)$). The proofs use it only through "infinitely many tame primes", enough for the $\mathbb{Q}(t)$ identity theorem.

The Lean statement 
def TameFunctionalEquation (F : DensityFoundation) (n : ℕ) (σ : FactorizationType) : Prop :=
  ∃ (num den : Polynomial ℚ), den ≠ 0 ∧
    (∀ q : ℕ, q.Prime → n < q → F.density n σ q = num.eval (q : ℚ) / den.eval (q : ℚ)) ∧
    IsPalindromic num den

lean/LeanUrat/Interface.lean — definition

definition The real order-0 density foundation

The instance the canonical capstone fires through, replacing a quarantined vacuous predecessor whose density was identically 0; its menu is the order-0 separable slice, so ramified types get an empty menu here by construction (see the empty-menu gate below).

The first non-trivial real instance of the density interface: its density is the $\sigma$-keyed order-0 engine menu-sum $\rho(m,\sigma;q'):=\sum_{T\in\mathrm{menu}_0(m,\sigma)}c_T(q')$, where the menu holds the order-0 separable cluster shapes of type $\sigma$ and $c_T$ is the proved engine evaluator (a residue-field choice polynomial times a volume factor). Nonnegativity is proved at every natural $q'$ (via necklace-count integrality), and the stratification identity is the exact multiplicity-times-volume split on this menu.

lean/LeanUrat/OM/RealInstanceV2.lean — instance

proved Decided density equals the menu sum of coefficients

The decomposition workhorse of the spine; the all-primes uniformity capstone fires this theorem directly (it bundles no palindromy), and every value gate certifies its instance through it.

For any counting model $M$ and Montes data $D$ over it, the genuine decided-limit density of type $\sigma$ — the $N\to\infty$ limit of $M$'s normalized decided counts — equals the finite menu-sum $\sum_{T\in\mathrm{menu}(\sigma)}C(T,q)$ of the per-shape coefficients at the model's residue cardinality. Proved from the partition law, the per-shape counting limits, and uniqueness of limits in $\mathbb{Q}$; no rationality is used.

The Lean statement 
variable {q n : ℕ} {M : CountingModel q n} (D : MontesDataV2 q n M)

theorem countingDensity_eq_sum_coeff (σ : FactorizationType) :
    M.countingDensity σ = ∑ T ∈ D.shapesOf σ, D.C T q

lean/LeanUrat/MontesV2.lean — theorem

conditional The count-native goal theorem (abstract spine)

The canonical spine capstone: it drops the measure route entirely, and it uses no axioms beyond Lean's core — every Montes/counting/tame input is a hypothesis or theorem, never an axiom.

Given a counting model $M$, Montes data $D$, a degree-$n$ type $\sigma$, and a foundation $F$ whose density agrees with the count-native menu-sum $g_\sigma(q')=\sum_T C(T,q')$ at every $q'>1$ (the bridge), plus the tame functional equation for $F$: there is one $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}\in\mathbb{Q}(t)$, denominator nonvanishing at all $q'>1$, representing $g_\sigma$ there, whose value at the model's own $q$ is the genuine decided-limit density, and which is palindromic. Palindromy transfers from the tame representative by the $\mathbb{Q}(t)$ identity theorem over the infinitely many tame primes.

Hypotheses this rests on: hbridge — the foundation's density coincides with the count-native menu-sum at every q' > 1 (discharged definitionally at the real instances); htameFE — the tame functional equation for F (feeds only the palindromy clause).

The Lean statement 
theorem goal_theorem_montes
    {q n : ℕ} (M : CountingModel q n) (D : MontesDataV2 q n M)
    (σ : FactorizationType) (F : DensityFoundation) (hσ : σ.degree = n)
    (hbridge : ∀ q' : ℕ, 1 < q' →
      F.density n σ q' = ∑ T ∈ D.shapesOf σ, D.C T q')
    (htameFE : TameFunctionalEquation F n σ) :
    ∃ (num den : Polynomial ℚ), den ≠ 0 ∧
      (∀ q' : ℕ, 1 < q' → den.eval (q' : ℚ) ≠ 0 ∧
        (∑ T ∈ D.shapesOf σ, D.C T q') = num.eval (q' : ℚ) / den.eval (q' : ℚ)) ∧
      (1 < q → M.countingDensity σ = num.eval (q : ℚ) / den.eval (q : ℚ)) ∧
      IsPalindromic num den

lean/LeanUrat/MontesV2.lean — theorem

conditional The measure-route goal theorem

The independent second route to the goal: a ∀-foundations statement (no constructed foundation is proven equal to the true p-adic Haar density — mathlib has no p-adic Haar measure); kept alongside the count-native spine, which carries none of these measure axioms.

For every abstract density foundation $F$ equipped with an OM-bridge (its per-shape cluster volumes realized by the fixed $p$-adic Okutsu–Montes cluster measure) and satisfying the tame functional equation, the per-type density $\rho(n,\sigma;\cdot)$ is represented by a single palindromic rational function of $q$ at every $q>1$ — wild primes included. Rationality is derived through the Igusa/Denef cell decomposition, never assumed.

Hypotheses this rests on: htameFE — the tame functional equation for F; the footprint additionally rests on the declared, cited axioms of the measure route (beyond Lean's core): the Denef–Igusa cell recursion and the OM-tree measure interface (cluster measure, cells, descent).

The Lean statement 
theorem goal_theorem
    (F : DensityFoundation) (B : OMBridge F) (n : ℕ) (σ : FactorizationType) (hσ : σ.degree = n)
    (htameFE : TameFunctionalEquation F n σ) :
    ∃ (num den : Polynomial ℚ), den ≠ 0 ∧
      (∀ q : ℕ, 1 < q → den.eval (q : ℚ) ≠ 0 ∧
        F.density n σ q = num.eval (q : ℚ) / den.eval (q : ℚ)) ∧
      IsPalindromic num den

lean/LeanUrat/Goal.lean — theorem

proved The density interface has a witness

A deliberately trivial witness — it is NOT the paper's real density (that identification would need the p-adic Haar measure foundation mathlib lacks); the same file also builds a coupled full-bundle witness with density ≡ 1 whose tame hypothesis is PROVED, firing the spine theorem axiom-free.

The abstract density-foundation interface is inhabited: an explicit witness (density $\equiv 0$, empty shape menus, cluster volume a fixed concrete cluster measure) satisfies all structural fields simultaneously, so the $\forall F$ goal theorems do not quantify over an empty type and the recorded measure identities are jointly consistent.

conditional The order-0 capstone at the real prime

THE canonical capstone since the 2026-07-21 rebase (its vacuous predecessor is quarantined); it uses no axioms beyond Lean's core, and the certified value is the order-0 DECIDED-stratum density — the tie to the full type density sits behind the exhaustive companion's open hypothesis.

For every $n>0$ and every type $\sigma$ of degree $n$, assuming the tame functional equation for the real order-0 foundation, there is one $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}\in\mathbb{Q}(t)$ such that: (R) the $\sigma$-keyed order-0 engine density $g_\sigma(q')=\sum_{T\in\mathrm{menu}_0(n,\sigma)}c_T(q')$ equals $\mathrm{num}(q')/\mathrm{den}(q')$ at every $q'>1$; (V) the genuine decided-limit density of the real counting model at $p=2$ — the $N\to\infty$ limit of normalized classifier-fiber counts of monic degree-$n$ polynomials mod $2^N$ — equals $\mathrm{num}(2)/\mathrm{den}(2)$; (P) $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ is palindromic.

Hypotheses this rests on: htameFE — the tame functional equation for the real order-0 foundation (certified a non-trivial constraint by a gate, but not yet discharged; it feeds only the palindromy clause (P) — clauses (R) and (V) are independently provable without it).

The Lean statement 
theorem montes_unconditional (n : ℕ) (hn : 0 < n) (σ : FactorizationType) (hσ : σ.degree = n)

      IsPalindromic num den

lean/LeanUrat/OM/RealInstanceV2.lean — theorem

conditional The order-0 capstone with the explicit full-density bracket

The guarded form of the full-density claim: it makes the decided-vs-full gap an explicit named hypothesis instead of papering over it.

On top of the base capstone's clauses, assume additionally that the normalized undecided mass drains: $\mathrm{undecided}_N/2^{nN}\to 0$. Then bracket uniqueness holds: any $d\in\mathbb{Q}$ lying in every normalized level-$N$ bracket $[\mathrm{decided}_N,\ \mathrm{decided}_N+\mathrm{undecided}_N]$ ($N\ge1$) equals the decided-limit density. This is the only definable full-density tie short of formalizing "the true factorization type over $\mathbb{Q}_p$".

Hypotheses this rests on: htameFE — as in the base capstone; hExhaust — the normalized undecided mass tends to 0 (true-shaped, numerics-confirmed, but OPEN: the order-0 classifier does not drain at any finite order — a target of the next work phase; discharged unconditionally only in the n = 2 uniformity capstone).

The Lean statement 
theorem montes_unconditional_exhaustive (n : ℕ) (hn : 0 < n) (σ : FactorizationType)
    (hσ : σ.degree = n) (htameFE : TameFunctionalEquation realF2 n σ)
    (hExhaust : Filter.Tendsto
      (fun N => (realM2 n hn).undecidedCount N / (M9.realP : ℚ) ^ (n * N))
      Filter.atTop (nhds 0)) :
    (∃ (num den : Polynomial ℚ), den ≠ 0 ∧
      (∀ q' : ℕ, 1 < q' → den.eval (q' : ℚ) ≠ 0 ∧
        (∑ T ∈ OMCountV2.omMenu n σ, OMCountV2.omCount T q') = num.eval (q' : ℚ) / den.eval (q' : ℚ)) ∧
      ((realM2 n hn).countingDensity σ = num.eval (M9.realP : ℚ) / den.eval (M9.realP : ℚ)) ∧
      IsPalindromic num den) ∧
    ∀ d : ℚ, (∀ N : ℕ, 1 ≤ N →
        (realM2 n hn).decidedCount σ N / (M9.realP : ℚ) ^ (n * N) ≤ d ∧
        d ≤ (realM2 n hn).decidedCount σ N / (M9.realP : ℚ) ^ (n * N)
              + (realM2 n hn).undecidedCount N / (M9.realP : ℚ) ^ (n * N)) →
      d = (realM2 n hn).countingDensity σ

proved The order-0 density theorem at every prime

The uniformity content at order 0, all degrees and all primes: the SAME rational function works at every prime — the general-prime, sigma-keyed strengthening of the p = 2 instance, with a companion positivity gate at every prime.

For each prime $p$, degree $n>0$, and type $\sigma$: one $p$-INDEPENDENT rational function represents the order-0 separable density family $q'\mapsto\sum_{T\in\mathrm{menu}_0(n,\sigma)}c_T(q')$ at all $q'>1$, and the normalized counts of monic degree-$n$ polynomials mod $p^N$ whose classifier lands on a menu shape (a genuine finite fiber count) converge, as $N\to\infty$, to that function's value at $p$.

theorem montes_order0_density_general_prime (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (n : ℕ) (hn : 0 < n)
    (σ : FactorizationType) :
    ∃ (num den : Polynomial ℚ), den ≠ 0 ∧
      (∀ q' : ℕ, 1 < q' → den.eval (q' : ℚ) ≠ 0 ∧
        (∑ T ∈ sepShapesOf n σ,
            rootCount M7.cellsOfShapeWF M6.treeSize M7.cellsOfShapeWF_descend T q')
          = num.eval (q' : ℚ) / den.eval (q' : ℚ)) ∧
      Filter.Tendsto
        (fun N =>
          (∑ T ∈ sepShapesOf n σ,
            (Nat.card {f : QuotientBox.monicBox p N n // B.classify p n N f = T} : ℚ))
            / (p : ℚ) ^ (n * N))
        Filter.atTop
        (nhds (num.eval (p : ℚ) / den.eval (p : ℚ)))

proved The all-primes uniformity capstone for quadratics

The paper's uniformity statement realized at n = 2: hypothesis-free and with no axioms beyond Lean's core (no tame functional equation needed, exhaustion discharged, no cited axiom in the proof's dependency cone); scope — fibers are digit-condition counting fibers (σ-keying faithfulness packaged separately) and palindromy is out of scope by the project lead's directive (these monic uniform densities are not palindromic); anchor gates recover 1/3, 1/3, 1/3 at p = 2 (tied model-level to the earlier machine-checked wild-prime model) and 1/4, 3/8, 3/8 at p = 3, checksum 1 at every prime, with an exact external per-family census at p = 2 and p = 3.

FIXED polynomials with no prime appearing in them — $\mathrm{num}_{\mathrm{ram}}=1$, $\mathrm{den}_{\mathrm{ram}}=X+1$; $\mathrm{num}=X$, $\mathrm{den}=2X+2$ for both inert and split — such that for EVERY prime $p$ (the wild $p=2$ included) and every degree-2 type $\sigma$: (R) the model's density family equals $\mathrm{num}_\sigma(q')/\mathrm{den}_\sigma(q')$ at every $q'>1$; (V) the genuine decided-limit density of the COMPLETE $n=2$ stratum tree at $p$ is $\mathrm{num}_\sigma(p)/\mathrm{den}_\sigma(p)$ — that is, $1/(p+1)$ ramified and $p/(2(p+1))$ inert/split; (B) bracket uniqueness holds OUTRIGHT, the exhaustion (normalized undecided mass $\to0$) being PROVED at every $p$ by a pure counting envelope $\le(\lfloor N/2\rfloor+1)\,p^{-(N-1)}$. The three densities sum to $1$ at every prime.
