lean/LeanUrat/MovesSp/SP6_exclusionLists.lean — theorem

proved The anchored residual is shift-invariant (the link to the moves corpus)

The corpus-link unit: it reuses the main moves corpus's anchored-residual predicate rather than duplicating it, tying the species bookkeeping (two coordinate systems, one anchor) to the carry-algebra development.

If a Laurent polynomial $x$ has anchored residual $R$ at anchor $a$ — that is, $x = z^a \cdot R(z)$ with $R(0) \neq 0$, the anchoring stripping off the power of $z$ — then $z^{\sigma} x$ has the same anchored residual $R$ at anchor $a + \sigma$. Consequently the residual partition $\lambda$, the selection, and $\ell = \deg R$ never see the relabeling $p \mapsto p + \sigma$ between the census and geometric coordinate systems.

The Lean statement 
theorem hasAnchorK_T_mul {K : Type*} [Field K] (x : LaurentPolynomial K)
    (a σ : ℤ) (R : Polynomial K) (h : Moves.HasAnchorK x a R) :
    Moves.HasAnchorK (LaurentPolynomial.T σ * x) (a + σ) R

lean/LeanUrat/MovesSp/SP1_shAnchorInv.lean — theorem

4 Mass laws on branches

The fraction of polynomials whose classifier run follows a given branch is governed by power-of-q weights read off the patterns along the branch, summed over the branch tree of the polynomial's factors. A first per-factor version of the law was refuted by a checked counterexample and rebuilt on the branch-tree architecture; the corrected law is proved in its abstract form, and its instances are verified computationally, the largest check being a 404,128,692-box partition census with zero double counts and zero orphans.

Shapes, charts and mass laws (MovesD) 7 definition · 5 proved · 8 conditional
Per-shape mass law: each shape's density is C·p^(−A′), machine-checked

MovesD proves the per-shape mass law: for each well-formed "shape" — the discrete skeleton of one run of the p-adic factorization classifier (per read: Newton-polygon side, factor degree, multiplicity, window position, residue-pool degree) — the number of degree-n coefficient boxes mod p^N realizing that shape obeys exact division-free counting laws, whose two constants (the class count and the digit-ledger exponent) are computed from the shape alone and are independent of the precision N beyond an explicit threshold. This is the counting engine behind uniformity: it converts each classifier outcome into a single monomial C·p^(−A′). It consumes the box-counting layer of MovesC (its Theorem C, part (b): the exact stratum-count identity) and the stage vocabulary of Moves, and exports the mass laws, the well-formedness gate, and explicitly typed interface obligations (a structural tree model, the canonical-policy specification, a threshold identification) to the tree-assembly corpus MovesT.

Builds on: MovesC, Moves

definition What counts as a shape: the twelve-clause well-formedness certificate

The subtype ruling makes well-formedness a matter of type: junk encodings lie outside every quantifier's domain, so the mass laws hold unconditionally over exactly the source note's shape universe.

A shape-prefix is a finite list of reads, each retaining the discrete data of one classifier step: its species (root, increment, or recentering), the Newton-polygon side in lowest terms $(e,h)$, the chosen factor's degree $g$ and its order $\mu$ in the anchor polynomial, an anchor offset $a$, the window position $(s_0, w_{\mathrm{side}})$, the frame width $D$, an intercept datum $u^*$, the total side weight $\gamma$, the residue-pool degree $w$, and a flag marking whether the side's top slot is the box's monic lead. The certificate is the conjunction of twelve laws every genuine shape satisfies: the root occurs exactly at read 0 and its side fits the degree-$n$ box; successive windows nest ($s_0^{(r+1)} + w_{\mathrm{side}}^{(r+1)} \le \mu_r$); $g_r\mu_r \le \ell_r$ where $\ell_r = w_{\mathrm{side},r}/e_r$ is the normalized side length; $e_r \mid w_{\mathrm{side},r}$; the frame-width chain $D_0 = 1$, $D_{r+1} = e_r g_r D_r$; the monic-slot tie (the flag holds exactly at a root read whose side spans the whole box); the residue-degree chain $w_0 = 1$, $w_{r+1} = w_r g_r$; the $\gamma$-tie $\gamma_r = e_r(\mathrm{STR}_r\, u^*_r) + (s_0 + w_{\mathrm{side}})_r\, h_r$ with stretch $\mathrm{STR}_r = \prod_{m<r} e_m$; and the Bézout anchor tie $e_r a_r = s_{0,r} - t^B(e_r,h_r)\,\gamma_r$, where $t^B(e,h)$ is the unique Bézout coefficient of $(e,h)$ in $[0,e)$. A shape of degree $n$ is by definition a certified prefix.

The Lean statement 
structure ShapeWF (n : ℕ) (P : ShapePrefix) : Prop where
  species_iff : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r < P.reads.length),
    (P.reads[r]'hr).species = ReadSpecies.root ↔ r = 0
  root_box : ∀ h0 : 0 < P.reads.length, (P.reads[0]'h0).s0 + (P.reads[0]'h0).wSide ≤ n
  window : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r + 1 < P.reads.length),
    (P.reads[r+1]'hr).s0 + (P.reads[r+1]'hr).wSide ≤ (P.reads[r]'(by omega)).μ
  gmu : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r < P.reads.length),
    (P.reads[r]'hr).g * (P.reads[r]'hr).μ ≤ (P.reads[r]'hr).len
  edvd : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r < P.reads.length), (P.reads[r]'hr).e ∣ (P.reads[r]'hr).wSide
  dchain0 : ∀ h0 : 0 < P.reads.length, (P.reads[0]'h0).Dwidth = 1
  dchain : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r + 1 < P.reads.length),
    (P.reads[r+1]'hr).Dwidth = (P.reads[r]'(by omega)).childWidthS
  monic : P.MonicTie n
  w0 : ∀ h0 : 0 < P.reads.length, (P.reads[0]'h0).w = 1
  wchain : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r + 1 < P.reads.length),
    (P.reads[r+1]'hr).w = (P.reads[r]'(by omega)).w * (P.reads[r]'(by omega)).g
  gamTie : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r < P.reads.length),
    (((P.reads[r]'hr).gam : ℤ) : ℚ)
      = ((P.reads[r]'hr).e : ℚ) * ((P.strS r : ℚ) * (P.reads[r]'hr).ustar)
        + ((((P.reads[r]'hr).s0 + (P.reads[r]'hr).wSide) : ℕ) : ℚ) * ((P.reads[r]'hr).h : ℚ)
  anchorTie : ∀ (r : ℕ) (hr : r < P.reads.length),
    ((P.reads[r]'hr).e : ℤ) * (P.reads[r]'hr).a
      = ((P.reads[r]'hr).s0 : ℤ) - bezT (P.reads[r]'hr).e (P.reads[r]'hr).h * (P.reads[r]'hr).gam

abbrev Shape (n : ℕ) : Type := {P : ShapePrefix // ShapeWF n P}

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/Defs.lean — structure

definition The class index: realized value-prefixes of a shape, up to canonical presentation

The class count is the numerator constant of the shape's density monomial — the quantity the mass laws and the uniform bound are about; the empty-shape dispatch bakes in the note's own convention that the empty prefix set is a singleton.

A history (a chain of concrete classifier steps) matches a shape $\hat P$ when its nodes agree with $\hat P$'s reads on every retained field. The prefix set of $\hat P$ consists of the histories that match $\hat P$, are coherent and realizable, live in the degree-$n$ box, and carry the canonical presentation (the policy's canonical lifts and the canonical residue root at every read). The $\eta$-class data of a history records, per read, the tuple of its side digits and the coefficient tuple of its chosen factor $\psi$, valued in the ambient finite field $F$; the class index is the finite image of the prefix set under this map. The class count is $C_{\hat P}(p) := \#\{\eta\text{-classes}\}$ on nonempty shapes and $:= 1$ on the empty shape, whose unique prefix is the empty chain.

The Lean statement 
def PrefSet (n : ℕ) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) (P : Shape n) : Set (History p F) :=
  {H | (P : ShapePrefix).MatchesHist H ∧ HistoryCoherent H ∧ Realizable H ∧
       InBox n H ∧ pol.IsCanonPres H}

abbrev PrefIdx (n : ℕ) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) (P : Shape n) : Type _ :=
  ↥(etaData (P : ShapePrefix) '' PrefSet n pol P)

noncomputable def Shape.CD {n : ℕ} (P : Shape n) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) : ℕ :=
  if (P : ShapePrefix).reads = [] then 1 else Nat.card (PrefIdx n pol P)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/Defs.lean — definition

definition Presentation data: one jet chart per class, plus the dictionary pin

The corpus's entire open surface is these two structure fields (the jet charts and the dictionary pin, both to be supplied by the presentation corpus HC2); everything else in MovesD is constructed or proved.

The corpus's only assumed data. A presentation of a shape at level $N$ assigns to each $\eta$-class a jet chart for its chosen representative — the coordinate system in which the box-counting theorem (Theorem C(b) of the jet corpus) counts that class's locus — together with the dictionary pin: (i) every policy-free realizable prefix is Galois-conjugate to some normalized class, and (ii) Galois-related normalized classes coincide. From a presentation one defines the class fiber $S(\eta,\top) \subseteq (\mathbb{Z}/p)^m$ (the representative's classifier locus at the full box, $m = nN$ base-$p$ digits), the event $S(\hat P,\top) = \bigcup_\eta S(\eta,\top)$ (the whole box on the empty shape), and the multiplicity $\mathrm{mult}(x) = \#\{\eta : x \in S(\eta,\top)\}$ (identically $1$ on the empty shape). A threshold datum is a presentation at every level $N \ge N(\hat P)$.

The Lean statement 
structure Presented (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (F : Type*) [Field F] [Finite F]
    (n N m : ℕ) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) (P : Shape n) where
  jet : (i : PrefIdx n pol P) → JetSetup (reprOf i) n N m
  hnorm : PresentNorm n pol P

structure Threshold (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (F : Type*) [Field F] [Finite F]
    (n : ℕ) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) (P : Shape n) where
  jets : ∀ N : ℕ, P.NP pol ≤ N → Presented p F n N (n * N) pol P

def Presented.fiber (S : Presented p F n N m pol P) (i : PrefIdx n pol P) :
    Set (Box p m) := (S.jet i).SHZ (topLocus p m)

def Presented.event (S : Presented p F n N m pol P) : Set (Box p m) :=
  if (P : ShapePrefix).reads = [] then Set.univ else ⋃ i, S.fiber i

noncomputable def Presented.mult (S : Presented p F n N m pol P) (x : Box p m) : ℕ :=
  if (P : ShapePrefix).reads = [] then 1
  else Nat.card {i : PrefIdx n pol P // x ∈ S.fiber i}

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/Defs.lean — structure

definition The ledger exponent and the level threshold

A′ is the exponent of the shape's density monomial; its level-freeness (proved in the threshold unit) is what lets one formula serve every precision.

The ledger exponent $A(\hat P,n,N)$ counts, summed over reads, the lattice points $(\text{level},\text{position}) \in [0,N)\times[0,n)$ lying in that read's fresh band — the region of coefficient digits the classifier pins at that read, reconstructed from shape data alone (lines, staircases, cumulative floors, rim thresholds). $N_{\mathrm{shape}}$ is an a-priori shape bound on the constrained levels; $A'(\hat P,n) := A(\hat P,n,N_{\mathrm{shape}})$ is the stabilized, level-free exponent. The threshold is $N(\hat P) := 1 + (\text{largest constrained level})$ when the shape has at least one class, and $1$ otherwise — the source note's piecewise convention.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def ShapePrefix.A (P : ShapePrefix) (n N : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.range P.reads.length).sum (fun r =>
    ((Finset.range N ×ˢ Finset.range n).filter (fun c => P.bandS n r c)).card)

noncomputable def ShapePrefix.Nshape (P : ShapePrefix) : ℕ :=
  1 + ((List.range P.reads.length).map (fun r => ⌈(P.lineS r).intercept⌉.toNat)).foldr max 0

noncomputable def ShapePrefix.A' (P : ShapePrefix) (n : ℕ) : ℕ := P.A n (P.Nshape)

noncomputable def Shape.NP {n : ℕ} (P : Shape n) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) : ℕ :=
  if Nonempty (PrefIdx n pol P) then (P : ShapePrefix).NPband n else 1

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/Defs.lean — definition

conditional Capstone: the per-shape mass law at every level past the threshold

The corpus capstone, consumed by the tree-assembly corpus (MovesT) to assemble per-shape masses into splitting densities; the proof is complete with no axioms beyond Lean's core, and all stabilization hypotheses are discharged internally.

Fix a nonempty certified shape $\hat P$ of degree $n$ and presentation data at every level $N \ge N(\hat P)$. Then for every such $N$: $\big(\sum_{\eta} \#S(\eta,\top)\big)\, p^{A'(\hat P,n)} = C_{\hat P}(p)\, p^{nN}$ (sum law, an equality) and $\#S(\hat P,\top)\, p^{A'(\hat P,n)} \le C_{\hat P}(p)\, p^{nN}$ (event bound) — the division-free forms of $\sum_\eta \mu(S(\eta,\top)) = C_{\hat P}(p)\,p^{-A}$ and $\mu(S(\hat P,\top)) \le C_{\hat P}(p)\,p^{-A}$. Both constants are computed from the shape alone and are the same at every level: one monomial in $p$ governs the shape's mass at all sufficiently fine precisions. When the shape has no classes, both sides vanish (the source's empty clause). A companion witness theorem constructs the threshold datum from a bare existence hypothesis for presentations.

Hypotheses this rests on: T : Threshold — presentation data (a jet chart per class + the dictionary pin) at every level N ≥ N(P̂); constructing this for the real classifier is the open HC-2 seam (the witness theorem reduces it to the existence hypothesis hex, PRESENT-EXIST, consumed only there). NP-ID (typed in NPv_npid.lean): that the formalized threshold coincides with the source note's own max-over-classes threshold is a declared open identification, deferred to the tree-assembly stage — the theorem's range is exact as stated; its identification with the note's range is the open claim.

The Lean statement 
theorem D4R4_all (hne : (P : ShapePrefix).reads ≠ []) (T : Threshold p F n pol P) :
    ∀ (N : ℕ) (hNP : P.NP pol ≤ N),
      ((∑ᶠ i : PrefIdx n pol P, Nat.card ↥((T.jets N hNP).fiber i))
          * p ^ ((P : ShapePrefix).A' n) = P.CD pol * p ^ (n * N)) ∧
      (Nat.card ↥(T.jets N hNP).event * p ^ ((P : ShapePrefix).A' n)
          ≤ P.CD pol * p ^ (n * N))

theorem TW_threshold_witness
    (hex : ∀ N : ℕ, P.NP pol ≤ N → Nonempty (Presented p F n N (n * N) pol P)) :
    Nonempty (Threshold p F n pol P)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/D4R4all_wrapper.lean — theorem

conditional The sum law: every class contributes exactly p^(nN−A′)

The heart of the counting: equal fibers via the jet corpus's Theorem C(b) at the full box, plus one shared shape exponent — this is where MovesC's counting engine is consumed.

At any level $N$ where the ledger has stabilized ($A(\hat P,n,N) = A'(\hat P,n)$, automatic for $N \ge N(\hat P)$) and for any presentation: $\big(\sum_{\eta} \#S(\eta,\top)\big)\, p^{A'} = \#\{\eta\text{-classes}\}\cdot p^{nN}$. Equivalently each class fiber has exactly $p^{nN-A'}$ points: Theorem C(b) gives $\#S(\eta,\top)\cdot p^{\#\mathrm{pins}(\eta)} = p^{nN}$ per class, and the pin-count transport identifies $\#\mathrm{pins}(\eta) = A'$ for every class. No disjointness of fibers is used anywhere.

Hypotheses this rests on: S : Presented — a jet chart per class plus the dictionary pin (the HC-2 seam data); hA — ledger stabilization at level N, discharged internally by the threshold theorems whenever N ≥ N(P̂); hne — nonempty shape (the empty shape has its own separately proved law).

The Lean statement 
theorem D4R1_SUM (hne : (P : ShapePrefix).reads ≠ []) (N : ℕ)
    (hA : (P : ShapePrefix).A n N = (P : ShapePrefix).A' n)
    (S : Presented p F n N m pol P) :
    (∑ᶠ i : PrefIdx n pol P, Nat.card ↥(S.fiber i)) * p ^ ((P : ShapePrefix).A' n)
      = Nat.card (PrefIdx n pol P) * p ^ (n * N)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/D10_sumLaw.lean — theorem

conditional The event bound: the union bound, division-free

The subadditivity half of the mass law; the incidence identity behind it provably FAILS on the empty shape (there the left side is the full box, the right side zero), so its nonemptiness hypothesis was forced by a compiled near-countermodel and is recorded as a deviation.

Under the same hypotheses, the event obeys $\#S(\hat P,\top)\cdot p^{A'} \le \#\{\eta\text{-classes}\}\cdot p^{nN}$: every event point has multiplicity $\ge 1$, so $\#S(\hat P,\top) \le \sum_x \mathrm{mult}(x) = \sum_\eta \#S(\eta,\top)$ (double counting of the incidence set), and the sum law finishes.

Hypotheses this rests on: Same as the sum law: S : Presented (HC-2 seam data), hA (discharged internally past the threshold), hne (nonempty shape).

The Lean statement 
theorem D4R1_EV (hne : (P : ShapePrefix).reads ≠ []) (N : ℕ)
    (hA : (P : ShapePrefix).A n N = (P : ShapePrefix).A' n)
    (S : Presented p F n N m pol P) :
    Nat.card ↥S.event * p ^ ((P : ShapePrefix).A' n)
      ≤ Nat.card (PrefIdx n pol P) * p ^ (n * N)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/D12_evBound.lean — theorem

conditional Equality criterion: exact counting iff every event point is covered once

The tie criterion: exactness of the density formula reduces to multiplicity one on the event — precisely the target the tree-assembly corpus (MovesT) must supply.

Equality holds in the event bound if and only if the multiplicity is identically $1$ on the event — i.e. iff the class fibers cover each event point exactly once: $\#S(\hat P,\top)\, p^{A'} = C\, p^{nN} \iff \forall x \in S(\hat P,\top),\ \mathrm{mult}(x) = 1$. The finite-level statement is pointwise and carries no measure-zero exception.

Hypotheses this rests on: Same as the sum law: S : Presented (HC-2 seam data), hA (discharged internally past the threshold), hne (nonempty shape).

The Lean statement 
theorem D4R1_EV_iff (hne : (P : ShapePrefix).reads ≠ []) (N : ℕ)
    (hA : (P : ShapePrefix).A n N = (P : ShapePrefix).A' n)
    (S : Presented p F n N m pol P) :
    (Nat.card ↥S.event * p ^ ((P : ShapePrefix).A' n)
        = Nat.card (PrefIdx n pol P) * p ^ (n * N))
      ↔ (∀ x ∈ S.event, S.mult x = 1)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/D13_evIff.lean — theorem

proved The encoding injection: a class is determined by its digits and ranks

The source note's L11 upgraded from claim to hypothesis-free theorem — the combinatorial core of the class-count bound, assembled from a chain of proved auxiliaries (digits lie in the pool, frame fields equal the pool, rank bounds and rank injectivity).

Each $\eta$-class of a shape $\hat P$ is determined by its encoding: for each read $r$, the tuple of its $d_r$ counted side digits — which provably lie in the pool $\mathbb{A}_r = \{x \in F : x^{p^{w_r}} = x\}$ of size at most $p^{w_r}$ — together with its selection rank $s_r < \bar m_r$, the position of its chosen factor $\psi$ among the eligible degree-$g_r$ factors of the anchor polynomial in a fixed lexicographic order. The map $\mathrm{enc}: \mathrm{Pref}(\hat P) \hookrightarrow \prod_r \mathbb{A}_r^{d_r} \times \prod_r \{0,\dots,\bar m_r - 1\}$ is injective — with no side hypotheses at all. At the empty shape the target is the empty product, of cardinality $1 = C_\varnothing$.

The Lean statement 
theorem encIdx_inj :
    Function.Injective (encIdx : PrefIdx n pol P → EncTargetP p F (P : ShapePrefix))

theorem encTargetP_empty (hP : (P : ShapePrefix).reads = []) :
    Nat.card (EncTargetP p F (P : ShapePrefix)) = 1

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/E9_encInj.lean — theorem

conditional The uniform class-count bound, with a prime-free selection factor

The uniformity input: the numerator of every shape's density monomial is bounded by shape data with a prime-free selection factor, which is what makes the density a single rational function of q across primes.

For every certified shape: $C_{\hat P}(p) \le M(\hat P)\, p^{W(\hat P)}$ and $M(\hat P) \le n^{|\hat P|}$, where $W(\hat P) = \sum_r w_r d_r$ is the pool-weighted count of retained digits, $M(\hat P) = \prod_r \lfloor \ell_r/(g_r\mu_r)\rfloor$ the factor-selection bound, and $|\hat P|$ the number of reads. The second inequality is prime-free — the uniformity-in-$p$ input. Proof: the class count injects into the encoding target (the encoding injection), whose cardinality is at most $M\cdot p^W$; at the empty shape both sides are $1$.

Hypotheses this rests on: hnorm : PresentNorm — the typed two-sided dictionary between the note's raw classes and the normalized presentation classes (totality up to Galois conjugacy + uniqueness); an open obligation to be supplied by the presentation corpus (HC2). The injection itself is hypothesis-free.

The geometric hinge of the corpus: a long proved chain (band transport, chart counting, shape replicas of lines/staircases/floors/rims) collapses here into one identity between history data and shape data.

The pin count is a priori history data; this theorem transports it read-by-read to pure shape data by matching the jet's fresh bands against the shape's reconstructed bands on the $N \times n$ coordinate grid. lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/G1_gates.lean — countermodel

definition The canonical-policy pin: what the one lift rule must satisfy

Interface typing: it specifies the note's one lift rule to the maximal extent expressible in exported vocabulary; the residual slot-selection law (WHICH weight-and-residual-equal realizer) is a NAMED open boundary to be discharged by the carry-algebra corpus HC1 — until then, a non-canonical but compatible realizer could also satisfy the specification, and the file says so.

The counting theorems quantify over any blind, deterministic lift policy; which policy is the note's canonical one is pinned by this typed interface. A pin for a policy consists of a predicate ('$t$ is the canonical offset lift realizer at node $\nu$' — whose exact formula is deferred to the carry-algebra corpus) together with five laws: totality at every recentering node; realizers are nonzero; a realizer's monomial support lies in a single arithmetic progression of stride $e$ and length $g$, anchored in the forced Bézout congruence class $j_0 \equiv t^B(e,h)\,\gamma \pmod e$ (with $t^B(e,h)$ the unique Bézout coefficient of $(e,h)$ in $[0,e)$, as in the shape certificate); uniqueness (exactly one realizer per node); and the digit-value law — at a recentering the realizer lies in the frame's coefficient space, carries the key weight, and its top graded residual equals the recorded center. Together these force every pinned policy's recentering lift to be a genuine, nonzero center realizer.

The Lean statement 
structure OffsetPPin {p : ℕ} [Fact p.Prime] {F : Type*} [Field F] [Finite F]
    (pol : CanonPolicy p F) where
  Realizes : Node p F → Polynomial ℤ_[p] → Prop
  total : ∀ ν : Node p F, ν.species = ReadSpecies.recentering → Realizes ν (pol.liftOf ν)
  realizes_ne_zero : ∀ (ν : Node p F) (t : Polynomial ℤ_[p]), Realizes ν t → t ≠ 0
  support_forced : ∀ (ν : Node p F) (t : Polynomial ℤ_[p]), Realizes ν t →
    ∃ j₀ : ℕ, (j₀ : ℤ) % (ν.e : ℤ) = (bezT ν.e ν.h * ν.gam) % (ν.e : ℤ) ∧
      ∀ j ∈ t.support, ∃ k : ℕ, k < ν.g ∧ j = j₀ + k * ν.e
  realizes_unique : ∀ (ν : Node p F) (t t' : Polynomial ℤ_[p]),
    Realizes ν t → Realizes ν t' → t = t'
  digits_prescribed : ∀ (ν : Node p F) (t : Polynomial ℤ_[p]), Realizes ν t →
    ν.species = ReadSpecies.recentering →
    inC ν.σ.Φ t ∧ ν.σ.w t = ν.σ.w ν.σ.Φ ∧
      ν.σ.R t = LaurentPolynomial.C ν.center * LaurentPolynomial.T 0

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/PolPin.lean — structure

proved Kernel refutations for the policy specification: degenerate and wrong-valued policies are excluded

The compiled refutations that killed two reviewer-found bypasses of the policy specification (a bare-nonzero instantiation and a singleton-realizer instantiation with wrong digit values) — each round of adversarial review left a compiled refutation behind.

Three compiled refutations guard the policy pin against degenerate instantiations found during adversarial review: (1) no pin's realizer predicate can be the bare nonzero predicate — the uniqueness law would force $1 = X$ in $\mathbb{Z}_p[X]$; (2) a policy whose lift at even one recentering node has the wrong top residual value (not the recorded center) admits no pin at all — the pin type is empty; (3) likewise for a lift with the wrong weight. Wrong-valued policies are thus excluded by type, not by prose.

The Lean statement 
theorem OffsetPPin.not_realizes_bare_ne_zero {pol : CanonPolicy p F}
    (pin : OffsetPPin pol) (ν : Node p F) :
    pin.Realizes ν ≠ fun t : Polynomial ℤ_[p] => t ≠ 0

theorem OffsetPPin.no_pin_of_wrong_value {pol : CanonPolicy p F} (ν : Node p F)
    (hν : ν.species = ReadSpecies.recentering)
    (hwrong : ν.σ.R (pol.liftOf ν)
      ≠ LaurentPolynomial.C ν.center * LaurentPolynomial.T 0) :
    IsEmpty (OffsetPPin pol)

theorem OffsetPPin.no_pin_of_wrong_weight {pol : CanonPolicy p F} (ν : Node p F)
    (hν : ν.species = ReadSpecies.recentering)
    (hwrong : ν.σ.w (pol.liftOf ν) ≠ ν.σ.w ν.σ.Φ) :
    IsEmpty (OffsetPPin pol)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/G2_gatesClassifier.lean — definition

definition The tree interface, structural half only: a declared boundary

The declared interface to the tree-assembly corpus (MovesT): only the structural counting half of the tree interface exists here — the stated cylinder and well-definedness properties are consumed by NOTHING in this corpus, the semantic child layer and the verdict structure are not declared at all, and the file says so explicitly rather than smuggling them in.

A structural model of the canonical tree $T_{\mathrm{can}}(f)$ attached to each coefficient box $f$: a membership relation between (possibly empty) history chains and boxes such that the empty chain is a member for every box (the root's presence is a law), membership of an extended chain factors through a child relation, and members are coherent, realizable, and canonically presented. Alongside it, two properties are declared but neither used nor proved in this corpus: the cylinder law — the class fiber $S(\eta,\top)$ equals $\{f : \eta \in T_{\mathrm{can}}(f)\}$ — and representative independence — any two histories in the same class present the same fiber under any jet charts. The tree's semantic layer (eligibility of children, verdicts) is deliberately absent.

The Lean statement 
structure TreeModel (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (F : Type*) [Field F] [Finite F]
    (n N m : ℕ) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) where
  mem : Option (History p F) → Box p m → Prop
  child : Option (History p F) → Node p F → Box p m → Prop
  root_mem : ∀ x : Box p m, mem none x
  mem_single : ∀ (ν : Node p F)
      (h1 : ([ν] : List (Node p F)) ≠ [] ∧
        ∀ (j : ℕ) (hj : j < ([ν] : List (Node p F)).length),
          ((([ν] : List (Node p F))[j]'hj).species = ReadSpecies.root ↔ j = 0))
      (x : Box p m),
    mem (some ⟨[ν], h1.1, h1.2⟩) x ↔ child none ν x
  mem_snoc : ∀ (H : History p F) (ν : Node p F) (hν : ν.species ≠ ReadSpecies.root)
      (x : Box p m),
    mem (some (H.snoc ν hν)) x ↔ (mem (some H) x ∧ child (some H) ν x)
  mem_realizable : ∀ (H : History p F) (x : Box p m), mem (some H) x →
    HistoryCoherent H ∧ Realizable H ∧ pol.IsCanonPres H

def D4R_CYL {n N m : ℕ} {pol : CanonPolicy p F} {P : Shape n}
    (S : Presented p F n N m pol P) (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol) : Prop :=
  ∀ (i : PrefIdx n pol P) (x : Box p m), x ∈ S.fiber i ↔ T.mem (some (reprOf i)) x

def ClassFiberWelldef (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (F : Type*) [Field F] [Finite F]
    (n N m : ℕ) (pol : CanonPolicy p F) (P : Shape n) : Prop :=
  ∀ (H H' : History p F), H ∈ PrefSet n pol P → H' ∈ PrefSet n pol P →
    etaData (P : ShapePrefix) H = etaData (P : ShapePrefix) H' →
    ∀ (J : JetSetup H n N m) (J' : JetSetup H' n N m),
      J.SHZ (topLocus p m) = J'.SHZ (topLocus p m)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesD/Defs.lean — structure

5 Transfer-matrix assembly

Densities assemble by a first-split recursion: condition on the root of the classifier tree, and each child poses the same counting problem at smaller degree with pattern-indexed weights. The depth-by-depth mass vector obeys a fixed linear recursion — a transfer operator with rational-function entries — proved as the abstract counting law and verified exactly at degree 3 through depth 2 against brute-force counts. Six named unproved statements remain in this corpus, each with a written record.

$$m_{N} \;=\; K(q)\, m_{N-1} + b(q)$$
The branch tree and transfer recursion (MovesT) 3 definition · 3 open · 9 proved · 5 conditional
Each decided polynomial grows one canonical tree; tree fibers get exact masses

MovesT is the branch-to-tree assembly layer: it defines the finite canonical verdict tree that records, branch by branch, how a p-adic polynomial's factorization is discovered, and proves — unconditionally — that every "decided" polynomial grows exactly one such tree. The pricing of trees is conditional: the set of polynomials growing a given tree has mass an explicit power of p, computed by charging each site of the tree exactly once (root split, unbranched segments, multi-branch splits) — but the root-split clause carries one fenced unproved goal (to be discharged by the HC2 corpus) and the per-site laws are consumed as an assumed ledger; a dictionary theorem then converts these per-tree masses back into the per-class masses the density tables use, under further named class-system hypotheses. The whole layer is exported as one named package of propositions that the density-assembly interface consumes, carrying both what is proved and what is open. Refutation records are kept public: a compiled countermodel showing the sibling-independence counting law is genuinely extra content, and two toy-model obligations recorded as false at their fixed toy tables (discharging them requires re-adjudicating the tables).

Builds on: MovesD, MovesC, Moves, MovesS, MovesSp, HC2

definition The finite verdict tree

The summation object of the whole corpus: densities are assembled tree by tree, so every counting theorem below quantifies over these.

A verdict tree is a finite set of nonempty branch histories (chains of factorization-discovery reads), closed under nonempty prefixes, together with: a multiset of root verdicts recording the unramified (Hensel) part, each of type $\{(e,f)\}=\{(1,g)\}$ (unramified of residue degree $g$); a verdict attached to exactly the maximal chains (the leaves); and a marking of some leaves as non-split ("ns") leaves. It is the finite combinatorial record of how one $p$-adic polynomial's factorization is discovered branch by branch.

The Lean statement 
structure VTree (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (F : Type*) [Field F] [Finite F] where
  chains : Set (History p F)
  hfin : chains.Finite
  hne_nodes : ∀ H ∈ chains, H.nodes ≠ []
  hclosed : ∀ H ∈ chains, ∀ H' : History p F,
    H'.IsPrefixOf H → H'.nodes ≠ [] → H' ∈ chains
  henV : Multiset Vd
  hhen : ∀ v ∈ henV, ∃ g : ℕ, 1 ≤ g ∧ v.1 = {(1, g)}
  leafV : History p F → Option Vd
  hleaf : ∀ H, (leafV H).isSome ↔ (H ∈ chains ∧ IsMaximalIn chains H)
  nsLeaf : History p F → Prop
  hns_leaf : ∀ H, nsLeaf H → H ∈ chains ∧ IsMaximalIn chains H

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/Defs.lean — structure

definition The fiber of a tree: which polynomials grow it

The graph-equality form of "the canonical tree of x is Tr"; its six clauses are what the uniqueness and mass theorems dissect.

Fix a counting model $T$, a chart $\chi$ embedding the $n$ coefficients into the $m$-coordinate digit box, and a digit vector $x$. The tree $\mathrm{Tr}$ "fibers" $x$ when six clauses hold: (i) the chains of $\mathrm{Tr}$ are exactly the nonempty branch histories realized at $x$, pruned at the first halt; (ii) each leaf carries the correct verdict — its branch halted with an irreducible factor, or it is a marked non-split halt; (iii) the root payload equals the unramified part read from $x$; (iv) no non-leaf has halted; (v) the degrees $e\cdot f$ carried by all verdicts sum to $n$; (vi) the root branches correspond exactly to the repeated irreducible factors of the mod-$p$ reduction of $x$, with matching degrees. "$x$ is decided" means some verdict tree fibers $x$; the fiber $\{x : \mathrm{Tr}\text{ fibers }x\}$ is the set whose mass all the pricing theorems compute.

The Lean statement 
def VTree.fiberAt (Tr : VTree p F) (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol)
    (χ : Fin n → Fin m) (x : Box p m) : Prop :=
  (∀ H : History p F, H ∈ Tr.chains ↔ (H.nodes ≠ [] ∧ PrunedMem T H x)) ∧
  (∀ H ∈ Tr.chains, IsMaximalIn Tr.chains H →
    ((IrrHalts H ∧ ¬ Tr.nsLeaf H ∧ Tr.leafV H = some (irrVerdictOf H)) ∨
     (NsHalts T (some H) x ∧ ¬ IrrHalts H ∧ Tr.nsLeaf H ∧
       Tr.leafV H = some (nsVerdictOf H)))) ∧
  Tr.henV = henPayload χ x ∧
  (∀ H ∈ Tr.chains, ¬ IsMaximalIn Tr.chains H →
    ¬ IrrHalts H ∧ ¬ NsHalts T (some H) x) ∧
  ((Tr.typemult.map fun ef => ef.1 * ef.2).sum = n) ∧
  (Tr.heads.val.map Tr.trackDeg
    = ((UniqueFactorizationMonoid.normalizedFactors (redPoly χ x)).toFinset.filter
        (fun ψ => 2 ≤ Multiset.count ψ
          (UniqueFactorizationMonoid.normalizedFactors (redPoly χ x)))).val.map
        (fun ψ => Multiset.count ψ
          (UniqueFactorizationMonoid.normalizedFactors (redPoly χ x))
            * ψ.natDegree))

def Decided (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol) (χ : Fin n → Fin m) (x : Box p m) : Prop :=
  ∃ Tr : VTree p F, Tr.fiberAt T χ x

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/Defs.lean — definition

proved Every decided polynomial has exactly one canonical tree

The ONE-F capstone: it makes the tree-indexed sums well-posed and is consumed by the class-tree partition and the dictionary sum.

If $x$ is decided — some finite verdict tree fibers it — then there is a tree $\mathrm{Tr}$ fibering $x$, unique up to extensional equality (same chains, same root payload, same leaf verdicts and non-split marks). "The canonical tree of $x$" is therefore a well-defined map on the decided set, obtained with no choice principle beyond the deciding witness and no counting hypotheses at all.

The Lean statement 
theorem oneF (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol) (χ : Fin n → Fin m) (x : Box p m)
    (hx : Decided T χ x) :
    ∃ Tr : VTree p F, Tr.fiberAt T χ x ∧
      ∀ Tr' : VTree p F, Tr'.fiberAt T χ x → VTree.ext Tr Tr'

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/F1_oneF.lean — theorem

proved Distinct trees have disjoint fibers

The unconditional partition kernel: uniqueness is read off the fiber clauses alone, which is what makes the one-tree theorem choice-free.

If two verdict trees both fiber the same digit vector $x$, they agree extensionally: equal chain sets, equal root payloads, and equal leaf verdicts and non-split marks on every chain. Consequently the fibers of extensionally distinct trees are disjoint — the tree fibers partition the decided set.

The Lean statement 
theorem tree_fiber_disjoint (Tr Tr' : VTree p F) (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol)
    (χ : Fin n → Fin m) (x : Box p m)
    (h : Tr.fiberAt T χ x) (h' : Tr'.fiberAt T χ x) :
    Tr.chains = Tr'.chains ∧ Tr.henV = Tr'.henV ∧
    ∀ H ∈ Tr.chains, Tr.leafV H = Tr'.leafV H ∧ (Tr.nsLeaf H ↔ Tr'.nsLeaf H)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/E9_fiberDisjoint.lean — theorem

proved The one-site mass step: passing a site costs an exact power of p

The per-site kernel of the whole assembly, proved from a new spectator-relativized counting law; the site ledger it consumes bundles per-site laws asserted as data (their discharge belongs to the classifier corpora), so applying it requires such a ledger to be built.

Let $H$ be a chain of the tree with ledgered site data (entrance event, state event, fresh coordinates, site exponent $e_H$). For every event $E$ that is a spectator of the site's fresh coordinates (membership in $E$ does not depend on them): $\#(\text{state}\cap E)\cdot p^{e_H} = \#(\text{entrance}\cap E)$. Passing a single tree site multiplies the conditional mass by exactly $p^{-e_H}$, uniformly in the spectator conditioning — the uniformity in $E$ is what lets the tree recursion condition on everything decided elsewhere in the tree.

The Lean statement 
theorem fiber_seg_step (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol)
    (CA : CellData p F n N m pol T) (Tr : VTree p F) (χ : Fin n → Fin m)
    (L : SiteLedger Tr T CA χ) (H : History p F) (hH : H ∈ Tr.chains)
    (E : Set (Box p m)) (hspec : SpectatorFor (L.freshCoords H) E) :
    Nat.card ↥(stateEvent T (some H) ∩ E) * p ^ L.siteExp H
      = Nat.card ↥(entEvent T χ (L.parentSt H) ∩ E)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/E6_segStep.lean — theorem

proved The open multi-branch pricing law already follows from the ledger's assumed faces

Added after an adversarial review found the tree-mass theorem advertised a pricing hypothesis its proof did not consume; the proofs were rewired to consume it and this lemma documents exactly where its strength actually lives.

At a multi-branch site, any site ledger discharging the presented faces (the exact per-site cell tie, freshness, spectator, and side-exponent laws) already satisfies the multi-branch pricing law at that site: $\#(\text{site cell event})\cdot p^{\sum_j e_j} = \#(\text{entrance event})$ over the per-side exponents $e_j$. So the pricing hypothesis that the tree-mass theorems carry explicitly is not an assumption beyond the ledger — its content sits inside the (still open) discharge of the ledger's own faces.

The Lean statement 
theorem presents_imply_jcMultiAt (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol)
    (CA : CellData p F n N m pol T) (Tr : VTree p F) (χ : Fin n → Fin m)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/E6_segStep.lean — theorem

open The root clause: the tree fiber splits along root branches

Everything except the single fenced hD4R0K clause is machine-checked (roster completeness is a proved consequence of (U)+(R)); the sorry site records the exact countermodel shape it fences and a fence-rule check that no in-corpus witness can refute or discharge it — this is the one admission all downstream mass capstones inherit.

Suppose the tree $\mathrm{Tr}$ is realizable and its fiber lies in the root cell $R_g=\{x:\bar f_x = g\}$ (digit vectors whose mod-$p$ reduction is $g$), with the chains of $\mathrm{Tr}$ split into $t$ root tracks (one per repeated factor of $g$). Then $\#\{x:\mathrm{Tr}\text{ fibers }x\}\cdot(\#R_g)^t\cdot p^n = p^m\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{t}\#(R_g\cap E_i)$, where $E_i$ is the event that track $i$'s subtree is realized as specified: conditionally on the root cell, the $t$ track events are independent and the fiber's mass is the product of the per-track masses.

Hypotheses this rests on: hD4R0K (a fenced unproved goal, to be discharged by the HC2 corpus): (U) two realized root children at one root-cell point sharing a track coincide, and (R) a realized root child's track is a repeated (multiplicity ≥ 2) factor of the point's reduction; plus explicit hypotheses hsib (the sibling count law), a full cell assignment with child covering, and the root/reduction cell laws.

The Lean statement 
theorem fiber_root_split (Tr : VTree p F) (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol)
    (χ : Fin n → Fin m) (trackOf : Node p F → Polynomial (ZMod p))
    (CA : CellAssign p F n N m pol T χ trackOf)
    (hχ : Function.Injective χ) (hrc : RootCellsOf T CA.toCellData χ)
    (hred : RedCellPartition T CA.toCellData χ trackOf)
    (hsib : SibCount T CA.toCellData χ) (hreal : Realizes T χ Tr)
    (g : Fin n → ZMod p) (htracks : RootSplitData Tr g)
    (hcr : ∀ i, ChildRoot none (htracks.headOf i))
    (hfib : ∀ x, Tr.fiberAt T χ x → x ∈ rootCell χ g) :
    Nat.card ↥{x | Tr.fiberAt T χ x}
        * (Nat.card ↥(rootCell χ g)) ^ htracks.t * p ^ n
      = p ^ m * ∏ i : Fin htracks.t,
          Nat.card ↥(rootCell χ g ∩ trackEvent Tr T htracks i (hcr i))

-- the single fenced admission inside the proof (the corpus's one assembly sorry):
have hD4R0K :
    (∀ y ∈ rootCell χ g, ∀ ν ν' : Node p F, T.child none ν y →
        T.child none ν' y → trackOf ν = trackOf ν' → ν = ν') ∧
    (∀ y ∈ rootCell χ g, ∀ ν : Node p F, T.child none ν y →
        2 ≤ Multiset.count (trackOf ν)
          (UniqueFactorizationMonoid.normalizedFactors (redPoly χ y))) := by
  sorry

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/E5_rootSplit.lean — theorem

conditional TREE-EXP: the tree fiber's mass is an exact power of p, charged site by site

The central pricing capstone, assembled from the root clause plus the per-site cone recursion after a review-forced re-keying of sites to their own branch-indexed reads (the earlier keying provably collapsed distinct sibling reads and is retired with the record kept in the file header).

$\#\{x:\mathrm{Tr}\text{ fibers }x\}\cdot p^{\,n+\sum_H e_H} = p^m$: the number of digit vectors growing the tree $\mathrm{Tr}$, times $p$ raised to ($n$ plus the sum of the ledger exponents $e_H$ over all sites of the tree), equals the full box $p^m$. Equivalently the tree fiber has mass exactly $p^{-(n+\sum_H e_H)}$, every site charged exactly once, the root included — the law making each factorization type's mass an explicit power of $p$ read off its tree.

Hypotheses this rests on: hD4R0K (inherited via the root clause — the corpus's single fenced admission); hjcm — the multi-branch pricing law at every multi-side site (its content is derivable from the ledger's presented faces — see the ledger-faces lemma above); hsib and hsibT — the global and per-site sibling-independence count laws (genuinely extra content: refuted at the bare cell-data layer by the compiled falsifier); L and sc — a site ledger and tree scaffold whose law fields (the exact per-site cell tie among them) are assumed data, to be discharged by the HC2 corpus.

The Lean statement 
theorem treeExp (Tr : VTree p F) (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol)
    (χ : Fin n → Fin m) (trackOf : Node p F → Polynomial (ZMod p))
    (CA : CellAssign p F n N m pol T χ trackOf)
    (hχ : Function.Injective χ) (hrc : RootCellsOf T CA.toCellData χ)
    (hred : RedCellPartition T CA.toCellData χ trackOf)
    (hsib : SibCount T CA.toCellData χ) (hreal : Realizes T χ Tr)
    (L : SiteLedger Tr T CA.toCellData χ)
    (sc : TreeScaffold Tr T CA.toCellData χ L trackOf)
    (hjcm : ∀ H (hH : H ∈ multiSites Tr T CA.toCellData χ L) (h2 : 2 ≤ L.sides H),
      JCmultiAt T CA.toCellData χ (L.parentSt H) H.lastNode (L.splitAt H hH.1 h2))
    (hsibT : ∀ H (hH : H ∈ Tr.chains),
      2 ≤ (CA.toCellData.branchSetOf (L.cellAt H)).card →
      SibCountAt T CA.toCellData χ (L.parentSt H) H.lastNode (L.cellAt H)
        (sc.splitFrame H hH).S)
    (hdet : ∀ H ∈ Tr.chains, ¬ Tr.nsLeaf H) :
    Nat.card ↥{x | Tr.fiberAt T χ x}
        * p ^ (n + ∑ H ∈ Tr.hfin.toFinset, L.siteExp H)
      = p ^ m

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/E8_treeExp.lean — theorem

conditional TREE-N: the mass identity at the standard working level

The finite-level mass identity feeding the density value; the genuine cross-level content that its removed threshold premise gestured at is the separate, still-open stability statement (next node).

At the standard working level $m = nN$: $\#\{x:\mathrm{Tr}\text{ fibers }x\}\cdot p^{A(\mathrm{Tr})} = p^{nN}$, where $A(\mathrm{Tr}) = n + \sum_H e_H$ is the tree's total ledger exponent. The density of the tree's factorization type at level $N$ is thus $p^{-A(\mathrm{Tr})}$; as proved the identity is threshold-free (an unused threshold premise was deleted at review).

Hypotheses this rests on: Identical to TREE-EXP (it is TREE-EXP specialized to m = n·N): hD4R0K inherited fenced admission, hjcm multi-branch pricing, hsib/hsibT sibling count laws, plus the assumed ledger and scaffold law fields (to be discharged by the HC2 corpus).

open TREE-N stability across precision levels (open)

An open row demanded by an adversarial review ("the mass identity alone is not level stability"); a recorded fence check shows no tower instance exists in-corpus so no countermodel is constructible, and nothing downstream consumes the sorry — the exported package carries the statement, not a proof.

This is stated in full and left open.

def TreeNStable {N₀ : ℕ}
    (Tat : ∀ N', N₀ ≤ N' → TreeModel p F n N' (n * N') pol)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/E11_treeN.lean — theorem

conditional The dictionary sum: class masses equal chain-count weighted tree masses

Restated at adjudication to live on the decided set — the "undecided set has mass zero" claim is a separate conditional statement deliberately NOT bundled here; the proof itself is complete, with no axioms beyond Lean's core.

On the decided set, for a fixed branch shape $\hat P$: $\sum_{\eta}\#\{x \in \text{fiber}(\eta) : \eta\text{ pre-halts},\ x\text{ decided}\} \;=\; \sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}} c_{\hat P}(T)\,\#\{x : T\text{ fibers }x\}$, where $\eta$ ranges over the enumerated branch classes of shape $\hat P$, $\mathcal{T}$ is a finite menu of trees partitioning the decided set, and $c_{\hat P}(T)$ counts the chains of $T$ of shape $\hat P$. This dictionary converts the per-class masses of the density tables into per-tree masses.

Hypotheses this rests on: hcyl — each enumerated class is the cylinder of its representative (note-proved; the Lean proof is explicitly owned by the tree/presentation corpus); hph — pre-halting is class data (to be discharged by HC2); hwd/heta/hib — class-fiber well-definedness, locality, and box laws; hsep — same-class chains on menu trees coincide; hpart — the finite tree menu partitions the decided set.

The Lean statement 
theorem preHalt_dict_sum {P : Shape n} (S : Presented p F n N m pol P)
    (T : TreeModel p F n N m pol) (χ : Fin n → Fin m)
    (hcyl : D4R_CYL S T) (hwd : ClassFiberWelldef p F n N m pol P)
    (heta : EtaLoc T P) (hib : InBoxOfMem T n)
    (hph : PreHaltClassData (P := P) pol)
    (𝒯 : Finset (VTree p F))
    (hsep : ∀ Tr ∈ 𝒯, ∀ H H', H ∈ VTree.chains Tr → H' ∈ VTree.chains Tr →
      H ∈ PrefSet n pol P → H' ∈ PrefSet n pol P →
      (∃ i : PrefIdx n pol P, SameClass i H ∧ SameClass i H') → H = H')
    (hpart : PartitionsDecided 𝒯 T χ) :
    ∑ᶠ i : PrefIdx n pol P,
        Nat.card ↥{x | x ∈ S.fiber i ∧ PreHalt (reprOf i) ∧ Decided T χ x}
      = ∑ Tr ∈ 𝒯, Tr.chainCount n P * Nat.card ↥{x | Tr.fiberAt T χ x}

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/F3_dictSum.lean — theorem

proved Pruned membership equals pre-halting for realized branches

The bridge between the model-relative pruned tree and the purely syntactic pre-halt predicate; every unit that trades chains against enumerated classes routes through it.

For a nonempty branch history $H$ realized at $x$: $H$ survives pruning at $x$ if and only if no proper nonempty prefix of $H$ has halted. The chains of the canonical tree are therefore exactly the realized histories all of whose ancestors are still running — pruning is a purely combinatorial condition on the history.

    PrunedMem T H x ↔ PreHalt H

Which digits matter is thus shape data, not class data — the identity making per-class thresholds computable from shapes.

conditional Every decided polynomial's tree is realized by a class-labelled tree

The rep-normalization premise was added only after a recorded necessity analysis showed the statement refutable without it (a realized chain differing from its class representative breaks the graph equality); with it the unit is fully proved, with no axioms beyond Lean's core.

The canonical tree of every decided polynomial thus lives inside the class enumeration, which is what turns the sum over trees into a sum over an enumerable index set.

The countermodel that justifies the premise rows of every mass theorem above: sibling independence cannot be derived, only assumed and later discharged by the classifier corpora — the falsifying model was itself hardened at review when its cell table was found to violate a newly mandatory uniformity field.

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/G1_toyGate.lean — countermodel

proved The pure-analysis leg of non-split vanishing

Proved in the definitions layer with zero admissions; its two companion legs (ns pricing, ns growth) are typed as named OPEN statements and never claimed — the explicit three-way split of the vanishing argument.

If a cap-mass sequence prices as exact reciprocal powers, $\mu_{\mathrm{cap}}(M) = p^{-Z(M)}$, the exponents grow, $Z(M)\to\infty$, and $\mu_{\mathrm{cap}}(M)\to\mu_{\mathrm{class}}$, then $\mu_{\mathrm{class}} = 0$. This is the analysis leg of the three-way split showing that non-split ("ns") halting classes contribute density zero; the pricing and growth legs are open, to be supplied by the classifier corpora.

    (hgrow : Filter.Tendsto Z Filter.atTop Filter.atTop)
    (hcont : Filter.Tendsto μcap Filter.atTop (nhds μclass)) :
    μclass = 0

Deliberately a set-inclusion, never a raw count claim — a refutation record in the source blocked the stronger counting form, and only the normalized domination corollary feeds the vanishing face.

lean/LeanUrat/MovesT/D12_packages.lean — definition

6 Tables and the resummation solve

The per-pattern weights are tabulated as rational functions of q, and summing the transfer recursion over all depths is a Neumann series: the candidate densities solve a finite linear system with denominators dividing an explicit determinant, verified nonzero at the evaluation points used. The degree-3 solve was re-derived bottom-up inside the development and matches the independently recorded tables exactly (all 65 decided integer values at both test primes; candidate functions sum to 1 identically). Four named unproved statements remain in the value-layer corpus.

$$\sum_{d\ge 0} K(q)^{d}\, b(q) \;=\; \bigl(I - K(q)\bigr)^{-1} b(q)$$
The rational-function solve (MovesS) 9 definition · 9 proved · 2 conditional
Resums the branching recursion into one rational function per factorization type

The solve layer of the pipeline: it converts the infinite-depth factorization recursion into finite linear algebra over the field Q(q) of rational functions in a variable q standing for the residue-field size. A transfer matrix per block size collects the one-step masses; inverting 1 - K resums all depths into a block solve, and a finite convolution over shallow shapes assembles the one fixed rational function R_sigma for each factorization type. Rigidity of rational functions - two that agree at infinitely many primes are equal - yields uniqueness of the interpolant and the checksum that all the R_sigma sum to 1. All 95 units (one stated Lean declaration per unit) are proved — machine-checked, with no axioms beyond Lean's core; the corpus imports nothing outside Mathlib, and its hypothesis structures (the measured counting ledger, the wild-pool read-off gate, the shallow-weight production law) are the explicit seam where the counting corpora (MovesT, MovesV) plug in and where the remaining conditionality lives.

definition Rational functions in q, with partial evaluation at a prime power

Every 'evaluate the symbolic density at a prime' step in the project passes through this partial evaluation; a pole surviving cancellation blocks read-off — detecting exactly this is the job of the wild-pool gate.

The symbolic carrier is the field $\mathbb{Q}(q)$ of rational functions in one variable $q$ (standing for the residue-field size). For each rational number $q_0$, the functions whose reduced denominator does not vanish at $q_0$ form a subring, and evaluation $f \mapsto f(q_0)$ is a ring homomorphism on that subring. Every passage from a symbolic identity to its value at an actual prime power goes through this partial evaluation; membership is after cancellation, so a removable singularity is harmless but a genuine pole blocks evaluation.

The Lean statement 
abbrev Qq := RatFunc ℚ

def OKat (q₀ : ℚ) : Subring Qq where
  carrier := {f : Qq | f.denom.eval q₀ ≠ 0}
  -- (zero/one/add/mul/neg closure proofs omitted)

theorem mem_OKat_iff {q₀ : ℚ} {f : Qq} : f ∈ OKat q₀ ↔ f.denom.eval q₀ ≠ 0

noncomputable def evalAt (q₀ : ℚ) : OKat q₀ →+* ℚ

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Defs.lean — definition

definition The transfer table: states, verdicts, and one-step outcomes

The shared vocabulary every theorem in the corpus is typed over; the verdict-to-(e,f) identification is what keys the densities to factorization types.

A transfer table for degree $n$ packages the finite combinatorics of one step of the factorization process: for each block size $e$, a finite set of states; a finite alphabet of verdicts, structurally identified with the pairs $(e,f)$ of positive integers with $ef \le n$ (ramification index and residue degree of a finished factor), the verdict's degree being $ef$; and for each state a finite roster of outcomes, each a nonempty list of members carrying a size, a base-change factor $\delta \ge 1$, and a status that is either a verdict (finished) or a state (continuing). With $m$ the number of members and $c$ the number of continuing ones, every outcome is dispatched to exactly one route: terminal ($c=0$), column move ($c \ge 1$, $m=1$), or split ($c \ge 1$, $m \ge 2$).

The Lean statement 
structure TableShape (n : ℕ) where
  State  : ℕ → Type
  fin    : ∀ e, Fintype (State e)
  deq    : ∀ e, DecidableEq (State e)
  VType  : Type
  deqV   : DecidableEq VType
  finV   : Fintype VType
  vdeg   : VType → ℕ+
  vEquiv : VType ≃ {ef : ℕ+ × ℕ+ // ((ef.1 : ℕ) * (ef.2 : ℕ)) ≤ n}
  vdeg_spec : ∀ v, (vdeg v : ℕ) = ((vEquiv v).1.1 : ℕ) * ((vEquiv v).1.2 : ℕ)
  Out    : ∀ e, State e → Type
  finO   : ∀ e (τ : State e), Fintype (Out e τ)
  odata  : ∀ e (τ : State e), Out e τ → Outcome VType State
  Wloc   : ∀ e (τ : State e), Out e τ → ℕ
  Wstate : ∀ e, State e → ℕ

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Defs.lean — structure

definition The transfer matrix of continuing moves

The object whose inversion performs the depth resummation; the entries' rationality is supplied by the rationality-burden interface, never assumed of the measured process directly.

For each block size $e$ the transfer matrix $K_e$ over $\mathbb{Q}(q)$ is indexed by the states of block $e$: entry $(\tau,\beta)$ sums the rational one-step masses of the outcomes at $\tau$ that are column moves (a single continuing member, necessarily of size $e$) with target state $\beta$. Powers of $K_e$ generate the multi-step masses of the depth process; the hypothesis feeding the construction — every column-move member has size exactly $e$ — is itself a proved unit.

The Lean statement 
abbrev KmatHyp (T : TableShape n) (e : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (τ : T.State e) (o : T.Out e τ), routeOf (T.odata e τ o) = .kcol →
    ∀ μ ∈ (T.odata e τ o).mem, μ.size = e

noncomputable def Kmat (T : TableShape n) {M : MeasuredSide T} (RB : RatBurdens T M)
    (e : ℕ) (hK : KmatHyp T e) : Matrix (T.State e) (T.State e) Qq :=
  fun τ β => ∑ o : T.Out e τ,
    if hk : routeOf (T.odata e τ o) = .kcol then
      (if kTarget T e τ o hk (hK τ o hk) = β then RB.TG e τ o else 0)
    else 0

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Defs.lean — definition

definition The block solve: resumming infinite depth by one matrix inversion

The central construction of the corpus: one matrix inversion replaces an infinite recursion; the capstone corpus (MovesU) identifies its density function R with sums of exactly these solves.

The block solve is defined by strong recursion on the block size $e$: $\beta_e(\tau,\sigma') = \big((1-K_e)^{-1}(b_e^{\mathrm{term}} + b_e^{\mathrm{split}})\big)_\tau$, where $b^{\mathrm{term}}$ collects the terminal outcomes emitting the verdict multiset $\sigma'$, and $b^{\mathrm{split}}$ collects the split outcomes, each contributing its splitting mass $J$ times a convolution, over decompositions $\sigma' = \sum_j \sigma_j$, of the already-computed solves at the strictly smaller member sizes, with each leg's variable rescaled $q \mapsto q^{\delta}$ by its base-change factor. One matrix inversion thus resums the entire infinite-depth branching recursion of a block into a single element of $\mathbb{Q}(q)$.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def blockSolve (T : TableShape n) {M : MeasuredSide T}
    (RB : RatBurdens T M) (hdc : DegCons T)
    (hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e) (hdet : DetHyp T RB hK) :
    ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → T.State e → Multiset T.VType → Qq

theorem blockSolve_eq (T : TableShape n) {M : MeasuredSide T}
    (RB : RatBurdens T M) (hdc : DegCons T)
    (hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e) (hdet : DetHyp T RB hK)
    (e : ℕ) (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n) (τ : T.State e) (σ' : Multiset T.VType) :
    blockSolve T RB hdc hK hdet e he τ σ' =
      ((1 - Kmat T RB e (hK e he))⁻¹ *ᵥ
        (bTerm T RB e σ' +
          bSplit T RB hdc e he
            (fun e' _ τ' σ'' =>
              if he' : e' ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n then
                blockSolve T RB hdc hK hdet e' he' τ' σ''
              else 0) σ')) τ

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/BlockSolve.lean — definition

definition The candidate density: one fixed rational function per factorization type

THE object of the uniformity theorem: the capstone theorem in MovesU pins its density function R to exactly this definition (the solve identification S.R = Rsh).

For a factorization type $\sigma$ (a multiset of verdicts $(e,f)$ of total degree $n$), the candidate density is the single rational function $R_\sigma(q) = \sum_{\hat S} W_{\hat S}(q) \cdot \sum_{\sigma = \sigma_0(\hat S) + \sum_i \sigma_i} \prod_i \beta_{e_i,\tau_i}(\sigma_i)(q^{\delta_i})$: a finite sum over shallow shapes $\hat S$, each weighted by its rational shallow weight and by the convolution of block solves at the shape's blocks, base-changed by the shape's $\delta$'s. This is the one fixed rational function of the uniformity theorem — derived from the transfer-matrix solve, not postulated.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def shConv (T : TableShape n) (M : MeasuredSide T)
    (RB : RatBurdens T M) (hdc : DegCons T)
    (hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e) (hdet : DetHyp T RB hK)
    (Ŝ : Shape T) (σ : Multiset T.VType) : Qq :=
  ∑ g : {g : Fin Ŝ.k → Multiset T.VType // σ = Ŝ.σ0 + ∑ i, g i},
    ∏ i, powSubst (Ŝ.δOf i)
      (blockSolve T RB hdc hK hdet (Ŝ.eOf i) (Ŝ.eIcc i) (Ŝ.τOf i) (g.1 i))

noncomputable def Rsh (T : TableShape n) (M : MeasuredSide T)
    (RB : RatBurdens T M) (hdc : DegCons T)
    (hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e) (hdet : DetHyp T RB hK)
    (F : ShapeFam T) (WshP : Shape T → PolyGeom) (σ : Multiset T.VType) : Qq :=
  ∑ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, (WshP Ŝ).val * shConv T M RB hdc hK hdet Ŝ σ

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/RshDef.lean — definition

definition The escape condition on an evaluated kernel

Part of the typed per-prime regularity gate: it is the hypothesis from which invertibility of the solve system is derived, and its general discharge at all pools is on the project's open mathematical surface.

A square matrix $A$ with rational entries satisfies the escape condition if all entries are nonnegative and $A^k \mathbf{1} \to 0$ as $k \to \infty$: the total mass remaining after $k$ steps of the process tends to zero, i.e. every trajectory eventually terminates. This is the per-prime analytic regularity input, playing the role of a spectral-radius-below-one bound without ever invoking spectra.

The Lean statement 
structure EscapeE0 {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] [DecidableEq ι] (A : Matrix ι ι ℚ) :
    Prop where
  nonneg : ∀ i j, 0 ≤ A i j
  escape : Filter.Tendsto (fun k => (A ^ k) *ᵥ (fun _ => (1 : ℚ)))
    Filter.atTop (nhds 0)

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Defs.lean — structure

definition The full inherited hypothesis ledger of the checksum

The single structure the checksum inherits from; its field list is the exact boundary of what the counting corpora (MovesT, MovesV) must still supply at general n (instantiated in full at the n=2 gate).

The complete set of conditions the checksum inherits, packaged as one structure: the nine measured counting laws of the cell/height ledger; the raw measured subtree values with their fixed-point law and height-independence; null-set exactness; the base-prime pools with escape packages at every all-active base prime and at every consumed base-change leg pool $p^{\delta}$; the wild-pool read-off gate at every consumed non-all-active pool; the set of factorization types of total degree $n$; the shallow-shape weight presentations with denominator regularity, interpolation, boundedness, and height/counting carriers; and the measured densities with their total-mass identity and the interpolation law tying $R_\sigma$'s evaluations to them. Nothing rides implicitly: everything inherited is an explicit field.

The Lean statement 
structure RS4Chain (T : TableShape n) (M : MeasuredSide T) (RB : RatBurdens T M)
    (hdc : DegCons T) (hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e)
    (F : ShapeFam T) where
  L : LedgerIV T M
  B : RS1Bundle T M RB hdc hK
  hns : B.nsNull
  PrimePools : Set ℚ
  prime_sub : PrimePools ⊆ M.Pools
  prime_base : ∀ q₀, q₀ ∈ PrimePools ↔ ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ q₀ = (p : ℚ)
  pools_e0 : ∀ e (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n), ∀ q₀ ∈ PrimePools ∩ allActivePools M,
    Nonempty (PoolHyp T M RB e (hK e he) q₀)
  legs_reg : ∀ p : ℕ, (p : ℚ) ∈ PrimePools → RegP T M RB p hK F
  legs_read : ∀ p : ℕ, (p : ℚ) ∈ PrimePools → ∀ δ ∈ consumedDeltas T F,
    ((p : ℚ) ^ (δ : ℕ)) ∉ allActivePools M → ∀ hdet : DetHyp T RB hK,
      LegAgree T M RB hdc hK hdet B.βmeas ((p : ℚ) ^ (δ : ℕ))
  Sigmas : Finset (Multiset T.VType)
  sig_exact : ∀ σ, σ ∈ Sigmas ↔ (σ.map fun v => ((T.vdeg v : ℕ))).sum = n
  WshP : Shape T → PolyGeom
  wsh_ok : ∀ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, ∀ q₀ ∈ M.Pools, (WshP Ŝ).val ∈ OKat q₀
  WshVal : Shape T → ℚ → ℝ
  wsh_interp : ∀ Ŝ (hŜ : Ŝ ∈ F.Sh), ∀ q₀ (h : q₀ ∈ M.Pools),
    ((evalAt q₀ ⟨(WshP Ŝ).val, wsh_ok Ŝ hŜ q₀ h⟩ : ℚ) : ℝ) = WshVal Ŝ q₀
  wshval_bdd : ∀ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, ∀ q₀ ∈ M.Pools, WshVal Ŝ q₀ ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1
  shDom  : Shape T → Set M.Hgt
  shEvtH : Shape T → M.Hgt → ∀ (q₀ : ℚ) (N : ℕ), Finset (M.Box q₀ N)
  visH   : Shape T → ∀ (q₀ : ℚ) (N : ℕ), Finset M.Hgt
  shEvt  : Shape T → ∀ (q₀ : ℚ) (N : ℕ), Finset (M.Box q₀ N)
  shWeightH : Shape T → M.Hgt → ℚ → ℝ
  shevt_grouping : ∀ Ŝ (q₀ : ℚ) (N : ℕ),
    shEvt Ŝ q₀ N = (visH Ŝ q₀ N).biUnion (fun h => shEvtH Ŝ h q₀ N)
  shDom_ne : ∀ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, (shDom Ŝ).Nonempty
  sh_realized : ∀ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, ∀ q₀ ∈ allActivePools M,
    ∃ N₀, ∀ N ≥ N₀, (shEvt Ŝ q₀ N).Nonempty
  shweight_card : ∀ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, ∀ h : M.Hgt, h ∈ shDom Ŝ → ∀ q₀ ∈ M.Pools,
    ∃ N₀, ∀ N ≥ N₀, shWeightH Ŝ h q₀ * (Fintype.card (M.Box q₀ N) : ℝ)
      = ((shEvtH Ŝ h q₀ N).card : ℝ)
  wshval_card : ∀ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, ∀ q₀ ∈ M.Pools, ∃ N₀, ∀ N ≥ N₀,
    WshVal Ŝ q₀ * (Fintype.card (M.Box q₀ N) : ℝ) = ((shEvt Ŝ q₀ N).card : ℝ)
  Rval : Multiset T.VType → ℚ → ℝ
  r_bdd : ∀ σ (q₀ : ℚ), q₀ ∈ M.Pools → Rval σ q₀ ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1
  decidedTotal : ℚ → ℝ
  x3_total : ∀ p ∈ PrimePools, decidedTotal p = 1
  rs1_equates : ∀ p ∈ PrimePools, (∑ σ ∈ Sigmas, Rval σ p) = decidedTotal p
  rsh_interp : ∀ σ ∈ Sigmas, ∀ p ∈ PrimePools, ∀ hdet : DetHyp T RB hK,
    ∃ hok : Rsh T M RB hdc hK hdet F WshP σ ∈ OKat p,
      ((evalAt p ⟨Rsh T M RB hdc hK hdet F WshP σ, hok⟩ : ℚ) : ℝ) = Rval σ p

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Interfaces.lean — structure

definition The wild-prime read-off gate

The guard at wildly ramified primes: it forbids silently reading off a symbolic value where a pole survives; vacuously discharged at the n=2 gate (every pool there is fully active), live at general n.

The read-off gate at one pool $q_0$ (a 'pool' is one evaluation site: a prime power $q_0$ together with its measured counting data): for every block size there exists a per-pool package under which the symbolic block solve, evaluated at $q_0$ after cancellation, agrees with the solve $\big((1-A)^{-1} b\big)$ of the active subsystem against the measured exit vector, at every active state, verdict multiset, and entrance height. At a wildly ramified (non-all-active) pool this check must pass before the symbolic value may be read off; a pole surviving cancellation there is a failure of the gate. Determinants of the inactive (junk) blocks are recorded but never required nonzero.

The Lean statement 
def LegAgree (T : TableShape n) (M : MeasuredSide T) (RB : RatBurdens T M)
    (hdc : DegCons T) (hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e)
    (hdet : DetHyp T RB hK)
    (βm : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → ℕ → T.State e → Multiset T.VType → ℚ → ℝ)
    (q₀ : ℚ) : Prop :=
  ∀ e (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n), ∃ P : PoolHyp T M RB e (hK e he) q₀,
    ∀ (τA : P.Act) (σ' : Multiset T.VType) (h_ent : ℕ),
      AVAgree P (blockSolve T RB hdc hK hdet e he (↑τA) σ')
        (bhatMeas P (fun e' he' => βm e' he' h_ent) σ') τA

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Interfaces.lean — definition

definition The shallow-weight production law (the named open burden)

The named open burden inside the checksum's hypothesis surface: its general-carrier discharge is this corpus family's single fenced unproved goal (a recorded `sorry` in MovesV/V7_w17ii, clause (ii)); the n=2 device instance is fully discharged. Keeping it explicit is what licenses calling the rest of the corpus proved.

The production law for the shallow weights — the checksum's one explicitly open inheritance: for every shallow shape in the family and every pool, (i) the shape's weight presentation evaluates to its weight value, and (ii) that value equals the sum of the shape's height weights over its height domain. The capstone consumes this as an explicit hypothesis and, by the repo's standing discipline, may not be recorded unconditional while the law is open.

The Lean statement 
def W17ii (T : TableShape n) (M : MeasuredSide T) (F : ShapeFam T)
    (WshP : Shape T → PolyGeom) (WshVal : Shape T → ℚ → ℝ)
    (shDom : Shape T → Set M.Hgt) (shWeightH : Shape T → M.Hgt → ℚ → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ Ŝ ∈ F.Sh, ∀ q₀ ∈ M.Pools,
    (∃ hok : (WshP Ŝ).val ∈ OKat q₀,
      ((evalAt q₀ ⟨(WshP Ŝ).val, hok⟩ : ℚ) : ℝ) = WshVal Ŝ q₀) ∧
    HasSum (fun h : shDom Ŝ => shWeightH Ŝ (↑h) q₀) (WshVal Ŝ q₀)

def RS4Chain.wsh17_pin {T : TableShape n} {M : MeasuredSide T} {RB : RatBurdens T M}
    {hdc : DegCons T} {hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e} {F : ShapeFam T}
    (C : RS4Chain T M RB hdc hK F) : Prop :=
  W17ii T M F C.WshP C.WshVal C.shDom C.shWeightH

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Interfaces.lean — definition

proved Rigidity: vanishing at infinitely many points forces zero

The corpus-wide workhorse: uniqueness of R_sigma, the symbolic row sums, the symbolic determinant, and the checksum all reduce to this rigidity over an infinite prime locus.

If $f \in \mathbb{Q}(q)$ has denominator nonvanishing on an infinite set $S \subseteq \mathbb{Q}$ and $f(x) = 0$ for every $x \in S$, then $f = 0$ identically. This is the engine converting agreement at infinitely many primes into identity of rational functions — the mechanism behind one fixed rational function serving all regular primes.

The Lean statement 
theorem ratfunc_eval_infinite (f : Qq) (S : Set ℚ) (hS : S.Infinite)
    (hden : ∀ x ∈ S, f.denom.eval x ≠ 0)
    (hval : ∀ x ∈ S, RatFunc.eval (RingHom.id ℚ) x f = 0) :
    f = 0

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/RatfuncEvalInfinite.lean — theorem

proved The one-step masses sum to one, symbolically

The conservation law (the note's K-SUB) that makes the solve a probability resummation rather than formal algebra; consumes the measured counting ledger and the rationality burdens as typed hypotheses.

For every block size $e \in [1,n]$ and state $\tau$, the route-dispatched one-step masses over all outcomes sum to $1$ identically in $\mathbb{Q}(q)$ (split outcomes contribute their splitting mass $J$, all others their terminal/column mass $TG$). Proved by evaluating at every all-active pool — where the measured row masses sum to $1$ by the counting ledger — and applying rigidity on the infinite all-active locus.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def routedMass {T : TableShape n} {M : MeasuredSide T}
    (RB : RatBurdens T M) (e : ℕ) (τ : T.State e) (o : T.Out e τ) : Qq :=
  if routeOf (T.odata e τ o) = .split then RB.J e τ o else RB.TG e τ o

theorem ksub {T : TableShape n} {M : MeasuredSide T} (RB : RatBurdens T M)
    (L : LedgerIV T M) (hdc : DegCons T) {e : ℕ} (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n)
    (τ : T.State e) (hK : KmatHyp T e) :
    ∑ o : T.Out e τ, routedMass RB e τ o = 1

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Ksub.lean — theorem

proved Escape makes the linear system invertible

Turns the per-prime termination condition into well-posedness of the resummation's linear system at each evaluated prime.

If $A$ satisfies the escape condition (nonnegative entries, $A^k\mathbf{1} \to 0$) then $\det(1-A) \neq 0$. For an empty active block the determinant is $1$ (vacuously nonsingular, matching the realized-states-only scope). Otherwise a vanishing determinant would give a nonzero fixed vector $v$ of $A$, and nonnegativity bounds $|v_i| \le (\max_j |v_j|)\,(A^k\mathbf{1})_i \to 0$, forcing $v = 0$.

The Lean statement 
theorem e0_det_ne_zero {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] [DecidableEq ι]
    {A : Matrix ι ι ℚ} (h : EscapeE0 A) : (1 - A).det ≠ 0

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/E0DetNeZero.lean — theorem

proved The symbolic determinant never vanishes

Lifts per-prime invertibility to invertibility in Q(q): the reason the block solve is well-defined symbolically and not merely prime by prime.

Given escape packages at all all-active base-prime pools, the symbolic determinant $\det(1-K_e)$ is nonzero in $\mathbb{Q}(q)$ for every block size $e \in [1,n]$: all but finitely many base primes are all-active, so an all-active base prime exists; if the determinant vanished identically its evaluation there would vanish, contradicting escape-invertibility. This discharges — from the ledger's own fields — the determinant hypothesis every solve consumer carries.

The Lean statement 
theorem rs3_det_symbolic {T : TableShape n} {M : MeasuredSide T}
    (RB : RatBurdens T M) (hdc : DegCons T)
    (hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e)
    (PP : Set ℚ) (hsub : PP ⊆ M.Pools)
    (hbase : ∀ q₀, q₀ ∈ PP ↔ ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ q₀ = (p : ℚ))
    (hact : ∀ e (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n), ∀ q₀ ∈ PP ∩ allActivePools M,
      Nonempty (PoolHyp T M RB e (hK e he) q₀)) :
    ∀ e (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n), (1 - Kmat T RB e (hK e he)).det ≠ 0

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Rs3DetSymbolic.lean — theorem

proved All but finitely many primes are fully active

Why one rational function can serve almost all primes: the finitely many exceptional (wild) primes cannot break an identity of rational functions, and at them the read-off gate takes over.

Among the base-prime pools, only finitely many fail to be all-active (i.e. have some inactive state). A state is inactive at $q_0$ exactly when some per-cell count polynomial vanishes at $q_0$; each such polynomial is nonzero, so it contributes only finitely many roots. This cofiniteness supplies the infinite identification locus on which rational-function identities are forced.

The Lean statement 
theorem allActive_cofinite_primes {T : TableShape n} {M : MeasuredSide T}
    (RB : RatBurdens T M) (PP : Set ℚ) (hsub : PP ⊆ M.Pools)
    (hbase : ∀ q₀, q₀ ∈ PP ↔ ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ q₀ = (p : ℚ)) :
    {q₀ ∈ PP | q₀ ∉ allActivePools M}.Finite

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/AllActivePrimes.lean — theorem

conditional The rational function is unique: any interpolant equals the solve

The uniqueness half of uniformity: whatever rational function the measured densities trace out, it can only be the block solve. The Lean proof itself is complete (no axioms beyond Lean's core); the statement is conditional on the named hypothesis surface.

On an infinite set $S$ of all-active pools, any family of rational functions that is denominator-regular on $S$ and interpolates the measured subtree values there is equal — coordinate by coordinate, at every block size, state, and verdict multiset — to the block solve: every $\beta_{e,\tau}(\sigma')$ is one fixed rational function. The identification reads the symbolic solve off against the measured values at each point of $S$ (through the read-off bundle) and then applies per-coordinate uniqueness of interpolants.

Hypotheses this rests on: hread : ReadOffBundle S hS B hdet — the per-pool active-value agreement (read-off) burden across S, a recorded open obligation (instantiated at the n=2 gate, open at general n); L : LedgerIV T M — the nine measured counting laws (a scoped binder confirmed necessary by independent review 2026-07-30); hdet : DetHyp T RB hK — nonvanishing of every det(1-K_e), dischargeable via the symbolic-determinant theorem.

The Lean statement 
theorem rs2_unique_interp {T : TableShape n} {M : MeasuredSide T}
    {RB : RatBurdens T M} {hdc : DegCons T}
    {hK : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → KmatHyp T e}
    (S : Set ℚ) (hS : S ⊆ allActivePools M) (hinf : S.Infinite)
    (B : RS1Bundle T M RB hdc hK) (L : LedgerIV T M) (hdet : DetHyp T RB hK)
    (hread : ReadOffBundle S hS B hdet)
    (f : ∀ e, e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n → T.State e → Multiset T.VType → Qq)
    (hfok : ∀ e (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n) (τ : T.State e)
      (σ' : Multiset T.VType) (q₀ : ℚ), q₀ ∈ S → f e he τ σ' ∈ OKat q₀)
    (hfin : ∀ e (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n) (τ : T.State e)
      (σ' : Multiset T.VType) (q₀ : ℚ) (hq : q₀ ∈ S) (h_ent : ℕ),
      ((evalAt q₀ ⟨f e he τ σ', hfok e he τ σ' q₀ hq⟩ : ℚ) : ℝ)
        = B.βmeas e he h_ent τ σ' q₀) :
    ∀ e (he : e ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n) (τ : T.State e) (σ' : Multiset T.VType),
      f e he τ σ' = blockSolve T RB hdc hK hdet e he τ σ'

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/Rs2Unique.lean — theorem

conditional The checksum: the candidate densities sum to one

The corpus capstone (the source note's RS.4): the resummation is exact. The Lean proof is complete (no axioms beyond Lean's core); by the project's standing discipline it may not be recorded unconditional while the production law is open — the conditionality is carried as the explicit typed premise h17.

$\sum_{\sigma} R_\sigma = 1$ identically in $\mathbb{Q}(q)$, summing over all factorization types of total degree $n$: the candidate densities form an exact probability distribution as rational functions, not merely at each prime. Proof: at every base-prime pool the ledger's interpolation fields evaluate the sum to the measured decided total, which is $1$; the primes are infinite; rigidity closes the identity.

Hypotheses this rests on: h17 : C.wsh17_pin — the shallow-weight production law (the source note's W17ii), OPEN: its general-carrier discharge is the single fenced unproved goal (`sorry`) in MovesV/V7_w17ii; the n=2 device instance is discharged; C : RS4Chain — the full inherited measured/rationality ledger (fully instantiated at n=2; its general-n instantiation is still owed by the counting corpora MovesT/MovesV); hdet : DetHyp T RB hK — determinant nonvanishing, derivable from the chain's own escape packages via the symbolic-determinant theorem.

The Lean statement

lean/LeanUrat/MovesS/N2WshpDevice.lean — theorem

Tables, ledgers and values (MovesV) 4 definition · 4 open · 6 proved · 6 conditional
The measured transition system: value tables, masses, theorem CTS-M

MovesV formalizes the value-table layer of the proof: it builds the measured transition system whose states are the classifier's branch cells (skeleton × digit cell) and attaches to every move the fixed symbolic value tables. Its capstone proves the twenty-clause measured-transition-system theorem (called CTS-M) FROM a substantial ledger of named hypotheses — table data, burden laws, three total-mass legs, and several open rows — concluding that at every admissible prime power the realized masses of the factorization process agree with evaluations of fixed rational tables: cell masses at an active state sum to exactly 1, entrance masses are geometric-series rationals, realized chain counts equal path-product polynomials, and the composition laws hold. All analytic content rides on a typed summability package supplied as hypotheses, and the height-domain factorization kernel of clause (v) (called HMC) is stated but never proved — every consumer takes it as an explicit antecedent. The corpus also keeps its refutation record in compiled form: three machine-checked countermodels that refuted the originally drafted species-word, height-writing, and template-totality statements (each replaced by a scoped-true repair), plus four named open duties fenced as the only remaining unproved goals.

Builds on: MovesS, MovesD, MovesSp, MovesC

conditional The measured transition-system theorem: all twenty clauses, given the hypothesis ledger

The corpus capstone: machine-checked as an implication (no axioms beyond Lean's core, no unproved goals in its own proof), with its conditionality fully displayed as the hypothesis ledger — three of the hypotheses (composite product, level stability, solve sentence) are named open rows, and the height-domain factorization kernel (HMC) is never proved anywhere, riding only as an antecedent inside individual clauses.

Fix a degree $n$ and a ledger $L$ packaging the transition system: the symbolic table family, the step system, its measured realization at prime-power evaluation points $q_0$ (the 'pool points'), the marked-event carriers, the analytic summability package, and the named burden laws (the polynomial value law, mark-fiber and domain ties, realization completeness, entrance count and uniformity). Assume the three total-mass legs (countable additivity, no mass at infinite height, fixed-height exactness), that every continuing marked cell carries mark weight $\geq 1$, the order-0 perimeter certificates on entrance domains, the composite product law, cross-level entrance-mass stability, and the solve sentence. Then all twenty ledgered clauses of Theorem CTS-M hold, among them: the state partition at each $q_0$ is finite with unique cell indices; every table value is the $q_0$-evaluation of a fixed polynomial, and the realized linked count $T_\gamma(x,h)$ equals the path-product polynomial on domain heights; no mass enters states inactive at $q_0$; entrance masses aggregate exactly and each is a rational geometric series in $q_0$; the cell masses at any active state sum to exactly $1$ (PART-1) and marked $+$ terminal $= 1 +$ surplus (PART-2; the surplus is the overcount $\sum(\text{mark weight}-1)\times\text{cell mass}$ from cells carrying more than one eligible mark); row values are independent of the history; events of distinct marks are disjoint; and the composition laws COMP-h/COMP-$\Sigma$/COMP-AGG hold, the latter two conditionally on the open factorization kernel.

Hypotheses this rests on: hb/hc/hd (the three PART-1 legs: countable additivity of cell masses, null remainder at infinite height, fixed-height exactness); hmark (every continuing digit cell has mark weight ≥ 1, the mark weight being the number of eligible marks the cell carries — refutable without it, an all-zero-multiplicity model breaks PART-2); hdom (every entrance-domain point carries an order-0 perimeter certificate — the scoping predicate defined in its own node below); hcomp (the composite marked-event value factorizes — a named open row, deferred future work); hstab (entrance mass is stable across levels — a named open row, deferred future work); hsolve (the solve sentence: determinant hypothesis + Neumann convergence, given escape/agreement/kernel/count premises); plus the ledger's own burden fields (value law VAL(a), mark-fiber tie, domain tie, realization completeness, entrance count/uniformity, stage laws)

The Lean statement 
theorem ctsM {n : ℕ} (L : CtsmLedger n)
    (hb : P1CtblAdd L.V L.X.w) (hc : P1NullRem L.V L.X.w)
    (hd : P1FixedHeightExact L.V L.X.w)
    (hmark : ∀ (τ : L.S.Cell) (c : DCellAll L.V τ),
      c.isLeft → 1 ≤ markWeight L.V c)
    (hdom : EntDomOrder0 L.V)
    (hcomp : CompProduct L.cc L.X.w)
    (hstab : IotaLvlStable L.cc)
    (hsolve : SolveCond_Stmt L) :
    CtsmConclusions L

The interface other corpora instantiate: proving the measured theorem for a concrete classifier engine amounts to building one such ledger, and its typed fields are exactly the conditionality inventory of the capstone.
