lean/LeanUrat/MovesU/DefsLedger.lean — structure

definition The prime-uniform finiteness kernel

Prime-uniformity enforced by binder order — one bound T(n,N) must serve every prime simultaneously, so no per-prime finite maximum can trivially satisfy the finiteness rows (the fix for an audit finding that the old bound was automatic).

The finiteness data of the tree-count argument, fixed BEFORE any prime is chosen: constants $L(n)$ and $D(n)$, a key-weight budget $w^*(N)$, and a tree-count bound $T(n,N)$, together with three counting laws holding at every prime: each stratum history has at most $L$ ladder restarts; on branches of decision threshold $\le N$, the non-genuine-increment nodes number at most $L \cdot D \cdot w^*(N)$; and the genuine increments number at most $n\cdot N$. Consumed per prime as: every threshold-$\le N$ tree slice has at most $T(n,N)$ members.

The Lean statement 
structure Cl7Kernel (n : ℕ) {C : UCarriers n} (KC : KernelCarriers n C) where
  L : ℕ
  Dden : ℕ
  wstar : ℕ → ℕ
  Tbound : ℕ → ℕ
  hL : 1 ≤ L
  hD : 1 ≤ Dden
  track_restarts : ∀ (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (f : MovesX.MonicBox n p)
    (H : MovesX.XHistory n), f ∉ MovesX.discZero n p →
    (KC.XF.gmn p).inStratum f H →
    MovesX.countPop H MovesX.Pop.recT1 + MovesX.countPop H MovesX.Pop.t4 ≤ L
  zero_gain : ∀ (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (f : MovesX.MonicBox n p)
    (b : (KC.XF.ctx p).Branch f) (N : ℕ), (KC.XF.ctx p).threshold b ≤ N →
    MovesX.dTotal ((KC.XF.ctx p).hist b)
        - MovesX.countPop ((KC.XF.ctx p).hist b) MovesX.Pop.incT12
      ≤ L * Dden * wstar N
  genuine_bound : ∀ (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (f : MovesX.MonicBox n p)
    (b : (KC.XF.ctx p).Branch f) (N : ℕ), (KC.XF.ctx p).threshold b ≤ N →
    MovesX.countPop ((KC.XF.ctx p).hist b) MovesX.Pop.incT12 ≤ n * N

lean/LeanUrat/MovesU/DefsLedger.lean — structure

9 The independent measure route and the trusted base

A second, independent route expresses the densities through p-adic measure theory and the Denef–Igusa cell decomposition (entering as a cited axiom, used on this route only). The root modules also hold the shared vocabulary (rational-function carriers, the goal interfaces) and the axiom census that pins the trusted base of every capstone.

The measure route and the trusted base (root modules) 2 cited axiom · 6 definition · 11 proved · 1 conditional
Shared rationality engine, counting lemmas, density semantics, cited-axiom boundary

This corpus is the engine room shared by both proof routes. It defines what "one fixed rational function of q" means, proves the abstract well-founded induction that turns any Okutsu-Montes-style tree recursion with rational coefficients into a single uniform rational function, and supplies the finite-field counting lemmas (squarefree count, Gauss necklace count), the Newton-polygon cell volume, and the palindromy-by-interpolation lemmas that the capstones consume. It also fixes the semantics of "density" (the limit of level-N decided counts, never the trivial box count) and the corrected nested self-loop recursion. Finally, it holds the measure route's declared analytic boundary — the cited Denef-Igusa cell-recursion axiom and the Guardia-Montes-Nart structural axioms, together with an explicit consistency model — and the axiom-census file that machine-prints every capstone theorem's trust footprint.

Builds on: Interface, L5fix, OM, MontesV2, Goal

definition What it means to be one fixed rational function of q

The target predicate of the entire program — every density capstone concludes that some counting limit satisfies it; the file also proves closure under sums, products, finite list sums/products, and division by pole-free denominators, all with no axioms beyond Lean's core.

A function $f : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{Q}$ is a *uniform rational function of $q$* if there is a single pair of polynomials $\mathrm{num}, \mathrm{den} \in \mathbb{Q}[t]$, with $\mathrm{den}$ nonvanishing at every integer $q > 1$, such that $f(q) = \mathrm{num}(q)/\mathrm{den}(q)$ for all $q > 1$. One fraction serves every prime power simultaneously — this is the precise form of the theorem's "independent of the prime" clause.

The Lean statement 
def IsRationalFn (f : ℕ → ℚ) : Prop :=
  ∃ num den : Polynomial ℚ, den ≠ 0 ∧
    ∀ q : ℕ, 1 < q → den.eval (q : ℚ) ≠ 0 ∧ f q = num.eval (q : ℚ) / den.eval (q : ℚ)

lean/LeanUrat/RatFn.lean — definition

proved The rationality engine: well-founded tree recursions have rational solutions

The central new inductive step of the paper: both the measure route and the count-native route are instantiations of this one abstract engine.

Suppose each "shape" $T$ carries a finite list of contributions — a coefficient function of $q$ paired with a list of child shapes of strictly smaller size — and a function $\mathrm{vol}$ satisfies, for all $q > 1$, $\mathrm{vol}(T,q) = \big(\sum_{\text{cells}} \mathrm{coeff}(q) \prod_{\text{children}} \mathrm{vol}(\text{child},q)\big) / \mathrm{pivot}(T,q)$. If every coefficient is a uniform rational function of $q$ and the pivot is rational and nonzero for $q > 1$, then $\mathrm{vol}(T,\cdot)$ is itself a uniform rational function of $q$ for every shape $T$. Proof: strong induction on size, closing each step with the rational-function closure lemmas. No prime $p$ appears anywhere in the argument.

The Lean statement 
theorem clusterVol_isRational
    {Shape : Type*} (size : Shape → ℕ)
    (clusterVol : Shape → ℕ → ℚ)
    (contribs : Shape → List ((ℕ → ℚ) × List Shape))
    (pivot : Shape → (ℕ → ℚ))
    (hrec : ∀ (T : Shape) (q : ℕ), 1 < q →
        clusterVol T q
          = ((contribs T).map
                (fun c => c.1 q * (c.2.map (fun ch => clusterVol ch q)).prod)).sum / pivot T q)
    (hdescend : ∀ (T : Shape), ∀ c ∈ contribs T, ∀ ch ∈ c.2, size ch < size T)
    (hcoeff : ∀ (T : Shape), ∀ c ∈ contribs T, IsRationalFn c.1)
    (hpivot_rat : ∀ (T : Shape), IsRationalFn (pivot T))
    (hpivot_ne : ∀ (T : Shape) (q : ℕ), 1 < q → pivot T q ≠ 0) :
    ∀ T : Shape, IsRationalFn (clusterVol T)

lean/LeanUrat/OMInduction.lean — theorem

proved Newton-polygon cell volume is a characteristic-independent power of q

The hypothesis records the cited Igusa box/shell Haar facts (absent from mathlib at the pin) in the project's recorded-via-hypothesis convention, so the closed form is derived (no axioms beyond Lean's core), never assumed — this is section 5 (BB1) of the human proof.

For a lattice Newton polygon $\Pi$ of width $e$, with integer ceiling heights $\lceil h_i \rceil$ over each column and vertex count $V(\Pi)$: if the Haar volume of the cell $\{g : \mathrm{NP}(g) = \Pi\}$ factors as the product over columns of the one-variable box/shell measures ($\mu\{v(c_i) = h_i\} = (1-Q^{-1})Q^{-h_i}$ at vertex columns, $\mu\{v(c_i) \ge \lceil h_i \rceil\} = Q^{-\lceil h_i \rceil}$ elsewhere), then it equals $(1-Q^{-1})^{V(\Pi)}\, Q^{-A(\Pi)}$ with $A(\Pi) = \sum_i \lceil h_i \rceil$. Both $V$ and $A$ are pure lattice data: a wildly ramified slope $a/b$ with $p \mid b$ gives the same $(V, A)$ as a tame slope of the same reduced fraction, so the cell volume cannot see $p$.

The Lean statement 
theorem cellVolume_eq
    (cellVol : LatticePolygon → ℕ → ℚ) (pg : LatticePolygon) (Q : ℕ) (hQ : 1 ≤ Q)
    (hfactor : cellVol pg Q = ∏ i : Fin pg.width, columnMeasure pg Q i) :
    cellVol pg Q = bb1Value pg Q

lean/LeanUrat/L4.lean — theorem

proved Squarefree monic polynomials over a finite field number exactly Q^m − Q^(m−1)

The complement count $Q^{m-1}$ of non-squarefree polynomials is the "needs refinement" residual count that becomes the genuinely q-varying factor of every wild cell coefficient — mathlib had no squarefree-polynomial count, so this is a from-scratch development.

Over a finite field $F$ with $Q = |F|$ elements, for every $m \ge 2$ the number of squarefree monic polynomials of degree $m$ is $Q^m - Q^{m-1}$. Proved from scratch via the unique decomposition $R = A \cdot B^2$ ($A$ squarefree monic, $B$ monic) and a strong-induction solve of the resulting convolution identity $Q^n = \sum_{2j \le n} S(n-2j)\, Q^j$.

The Lean statement 
theorem card_squarefreeMonicDegree (m : ℕ) (hm : 2 ≤ m) :
    Nat.card (squarefreeMonicDegree F m) = Nat.card F ^ m - Nat.card F ^ (m - 1)

lean/LeanUrat/L3Squarefree.lean — theorem

proved The Gauss necklace count of irreducible polynomials

The base of the residue-field combinatorics: it makes the tame shape counts universal polynomials in $Q$ (section 4 of the human proof) and feeds the order-0 counting instances in the OM corpus.

Over $\mathbb{F}_Q$, for $d \ge 1$, the number $N_d$ of monic irreducible polynomials of degree $d$ satisfies $d \cdot N_d = \sum_{e \mid d} \mu(e)\, Q^{d/e}$. Proved via the identity $\sum_{e \mid d} e \cdot N_e = Q^d$ (counting the elements of $\mathbb{F}_{Q^d}$ by their minimal polynomials) followed by Möbius inversion.

The Lean statement 
theorem gauss_necklace_count
    (F : Type*) [Field F] [Finite F] (d : ℕ) (hd : 1 ≤ d) :
    (d : ℚ) * (Nat.card (L3.monicIrreducibleDegree F d) : ℚ)
      = ∑ e ∈ d.divisors,
          ((ArithmeticFunction.moebius e : ℤ) : ℚ) * (Nat.card F : ℚ) ^ (d / e)

lean/LeanUrat/L3Gauss.lean — theorem

proved Interpolation from tame primes: one representative rules all primes

Section 12 (L7) of the human proof: whatever holds of the density's representative at the infinitely many easy (tame) primes is forced upon its behavior at the hard (wild) primes as well.

Suppose $S = \mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ represents the density $\rho(n,\sigma;q)$ at every $q > 1$ (denominator pole-free there), and $R = \mathrm{tnum}/\mathrm{tden}$ represents the same density at all tame primes $q = p > n$. Then $\mathrm{num} \cdot \mathrm{tden} = \mathrm{tnum} \cdot \mathrm{den}$, i.e. $S = R$ in $\mathbb{Q}(t)$: the tame primes are infinite (Euclid), so the identity theorem for polynomials — a nonzero polynomial has finitely many roots — forces the two rational functions to coincide.

The Lean statement 
theorem tame_to_all_primes
    (F : DensityFoundation) (n : ℕ) (σ : FactorizationType) (hσ : σ.degree = n)
    (num den tnum tden : Polynomial ℚ)
    (hden0 : den ≠ 0) (htden0 : tden ≠ 0)
    (hL6 : ∀ q : ℕ, 1 < q → den.eval (q : ℚ) ≠ 0 ∧ F.density n σ q = num.eval (q : ℚ) / den.eval (q : ℚ))
    (htame : ∀ q : ℕ, q.Prime → n < q →
      F.density n σ q = tnum.eval (q : ℚ) / tden.eval (q : ℚ)) :
    num * tden = tnum * den

lean/LeanUrat/L7.lean — theorem

proved Palindromy transfers along agreement of rational functions

This lemma is fully proved, but its tame palindromy input is exactly the capstone hypothesis that remains undischarged on the real instance — so in the current chain the functional-equation clause is conditional even though this transfer step is not.

If $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ and $\mathrm{tnum}/\mathrm{tden}$ agree in $\mathbb{Q}(t)$ (cross-multiplied form) and $\mathrm{tnum}/\mathrm{tden}$ is palindromic — satisfying $R(1/x) = R(x)$ — then $\mathrm{num}/\mathrm{den}$ is palindromic. The pointwise transfer fails at poles of $\mathrm{tden}$, so the proof establishes the *polynomial* identity $\widetilde{\mathrm{num}} \cdot \mathrm{den} = \mathrm{num} \cdot \widetilde{\mathrm{den}}$ (tildes = coefficient reversal) on a cofinite set and invokes the identity theorem, making it valid everywhere.

The Lean statement 
theorem isPalindromic_of_agree
    (num den tnum tden : Polynomial ℚ)
    (hden : den ≠ 0) (htden : tden ≠ 0)
    (hagree : num * tden = tnum * den)
    (htpalin : IsPalindromic tnum tden) :
    IsPalindromic num den

lean/LeanUrat/L7.lean — theorem

definition The order-r Okutsu-Montes shape datum, with anti-caricature ties

The proof-carrying fields exist to block caricature instantiations flagged in an independent audit: with them, |F_r| = q^{residueDeg} is a proved lemma rather than an assumption, and every downstream count is computed from a genuine polynomial over a genuine field extension.

A shape records the data of one node of the Montes tree at order $r$: a finite residue field $F_r$ with a genuine algebra structure over the base residue field — so the residue degree is the true extension degree $[F_r : \mathbb{F}]$, not a free integer — the actual order-$r$ residual polynomial over $F_r$ with its degree field tied to the polynomial's real degree, the order-$r$ lattice Newton polygon, and the cluster size that serves as the well-founded descent measure.

The Lean statement 
structure OMShape where
  size : ℕ
  order : ℕ
  Fr : Type
  [instField : Field Fr]
  [instFinite : Finite Fr]
  baseField : Type
  [instBaseField : Field baseField]
  [instBaseFinite : Finite baseField]
  [instAlg : Algebra baseField Fr]
  residueDeg : ℕ
  hResidueDeg : residueDeg = Module.finrank baseField Fr
  polygon : L4.LatticePolygon
  residual : Polynomial Fr
  residualDeg : ℕ
  hResidualDeg : residualDeg = residual.natDegree

lean/LeanUrat/PadicMeasure.lean — structure

cited axiom The Guardia-Montes-Nart structural interface (cited axioms)

Used by the measure route only (the count-native capstones avoid them); the once-axiomatic child-factor tie was demoted to a proved projection lemma by encoding its witnesses in the cell data, and the remaining open gap — that the cell strata reconstruct the full residual — is documented in-file.

Three structural facts about the Okutsu-Montes tree, cited from Guardia-Montes-Nart (Trans. AMS 364 (2012)), are declared as axioms: every shape has a finite list of order-$r$ cells (one per Newton-polygon face × residual stratum — the GMN cell partition); a descent operation producing child shapes; and the strict descent law that every descent child of every cell has strictly smaller cluster size (the well-foundedness input). None asserts any volume, rationality, uniformity, or $p$-independence — those are always derived.

The Lean statement 
axiom omCells : (T : OMShape) → List (OMCell T)

axiom descend : OMShape → List OMShape

axiom descend_size_lt :
    ∀ (T : OMShape), ∀ c ∈ omCells T, ∀ ch ∈ c.children, ch.node.size < T.size

lean/LeanUrat/PadicMeasure.lean — definition

cited axiom The Denef-Igusa cell recursion for the p-adic cluster measure (cited axiom)

The history matters: an earlier free-binder form of this axiom was proven logically inconsistent, and a companion axiom was found false-as-stated and deleted (2026-07-02) — which is why the surviving axiom is stated about one fixed object and shipped with a satisfiability model.

An opaque fixed symbol denotes THE conditional $p$-adic Haar cluster measure of each shape (mathlib has no $p$-adic Haar measure, so the object itself is an interface axiom), and the load-bearing analytic axiom asserts the Igusa/Denef cell-decomposition identity for it: for $q > 1$ the cluster measure equals $\big(\sum_{\text{cells}} (\text{residual point count}) \times (\text{Newton-box volume}) \times \prod_{\text{children}} (\text{child cluster measure})\big)$ divided by the geometric self-loop pivot $1 - q^{-w}$. This is a structural measure factorization, never a rationality claim. Citations: Igusa, Local Zeta Functions §7.4; Denef, Invent. Math. 77 (1984).

The Lean statement 
axiom clusterMeasure : OMShape → ℕ → ℚ

axiom AX_cellRecursion (T : OMShape) (q : ℕ) (hq : 1 < q) :
    clusterMeasure T q
      = ((omCells T).map (fun c =>
            residualCountFn T c.residualDeg q
            * L4.bb1Value c.polygon q
            * (c.children.map (fun ch => clusterMeasure ch.node q)).prod)).sum
        / omPivot T q

lean/LeanUrat/PadicMeasure.lean — theorem

proved The cell-recursion axiom is satisfiable: an explicit model

An artifact of the 2026-06-19 independent verification: it certifies that the current axiom form was adopted precisely because the old one was machine-shown inconsistent — the project's own record of catching a false axiom.

An explicitly constructed function — defined by well-founded recursion on cluster size, descending through the strict-descent axiom — satisfies exactly the identity that the cell-recursion axiom asserts. Exhibiting this model shows the axiom is satisfiable, hence relatively consistent: the fixed-object formulation cannot derive a contradiction the way its predecessor (with a freely quantified pivot, which forced $1 = 1/2$) provably could.

The Lean statement 
theorem clusterMeasureModel_rec (T : OMShape) (q : ℕ) (hq : 1 < q) :
    clusterMeasureModel T q
      = ((omCells T).map (fun c =>
            residualCountFn T c.residualDeg q
            * L4.bb1Value c.polygon q
            * (c.children.map (fun ch => clusterMeasureModel ch.node q)).prod)).sum
        / omPivot T q

lean/LeanUrat/PadicMeasure.lean — theorem

conditional Measure route: every cluster volume is one rational function of q

The complete but axiom-resting second route to rationality: it is independent of the canonical count-native chain, whose capstones deliberately avoid every axiom named in this gate.

The fixed conditional $p$-adic cluster measure of every Okutsu-Montes shape is a uniform rational function of $q$. Proof: the cited cell recursion discharges the recursion hypothesis of the abstract rationality engine, whose other inputs are all proved — strict descent from the GMN axiom, coefficient rationality from the finite-field residual count times the Newton-box volume, and pole-freeness of the self-loop pivot. Rationality is never assumed in any axiom; it emerges only through the induction.

Hypotheses this rests on: clusterMeasure — the opaque cited p-adic Haar cluster-measure interface object; AX_cellRecursion — the Denef-Igusa cell-decomposition identity for it (cited); omCells / descend_size_lt — the GMN finite cell partition and strict size descent (cited)

The Lean statement 
theorem clusterMeasure_isRational_of_AX :
    ∀ T : OMShape, IsRationalFn (clusterMeasure T)

lean/LeanUrat/PadicMeasure.lean — theorem

definition The count-native cluster recursion (no measure axioms anywhere)

The shared engine primitive of the count-native route: because it is built only from proved blocks, the density capstones that use it keep an axiom footprint of Lean's core only — the whole point of the 2026-06-20 re-architecture away from the missing p-adic measure foundation.

A cluster-count function defined by well-founded recursion on tree size: $\mathrm{count}(T, q) = \big(\sum_{\text{cells } c} (q^{\delta})^{d_S - 1} \cdot \mathrm{box}(c, q) \cdot \prod_{\text{children}} \mathrm{count}(\cdot, q)\big) / \big(1 - q^{-w(s)}\big)$, where $(q^\delta)^{d_S-1}$ is the proved non-squarefree residual count over $\mathbb{F}_{q^\delta}$, $\mathrm{box}$ is the proved Newton-box value, and $w(s) = s(s{+}1)/2 - 1$ is the self-loop exponent. Built entirely from proved counting blocks — no opaque Haar object appears.

The Lean statement 
noncomputable def clusterCount
    (cells : ClusterShape → List CountCell) (treeSize : ClusterShape → ℕ)
    (hdesc : ∀ (T : ClusterShape), ∀ c ∈ cells T, ∀ ch ∈ c.children, treeSize ch < treeSize T)
    (T : ClusterShape) (q : ℕ) : ℚ :=
  ((cells T).attach.map (fun c =>
      countCellCoeff c.1 q
        * (c.1.children.attach.map (fun ch => clusterCount cells treeSize hdesc ch.1 q)).prod)).sum
    / countPivot (treeSize T) q

lean/LeanUrat/MontesAxiom.lean — definition

proved The refinement-triggering configurations number exactly Q^(d−1)

This genuine finite-field count is the q-varying wild-mechanism factor inside every cell coefficient — an earlier audit found the wild count frozen to a constant (so the wild mechanism was never exercised), and this theorem is the repair that makes it a real count rather than a free monomial.

Over any finite field $F$, the number of non-squarefree (i.e. refinement-triggering) monic polynomials of degree $d_S \ge 2$ is $|F|^{d_S - 1}$ — the difference between the monic count $Q^{d_S}$ and the squarefree count $Q^{d_S} - Q^{d_S - 1}$.

The Lean statement 
theorem residualBoxCount (F : Type*) [Field F] [Finite F] (dS : ℕ) (hd : 2 ≤ dS) :
    Nat.card {R : Polynomial F // R.Monic ∧ R.natDegree = dS ∧ ¬ Squarefree R}
      = Nat.card F ^ (dS - 1)

lean/LeanUrat/MontesAxiom.lean — theorem

definition What "the density" means: the decided-count model

The semantic heart of the counting route: it fixes the key point that a density is the limit of certified counts, and realizing this structure with genuine p-adic digit counts is exactly what the real instances in the OM corpus do.

A counting model at residue size $q$ and degree $n$ carries, for each factorization type $\sigma$ and precision $N$: the number of coefficient boxes mod $p^N$ whose type is already *certified* by their digits (decided), and the number still undecided; subject to the constraints that decided plus undecided partition the full box $q^{nN}$, the normalized decided counts are monotone in $N$ and converge to a rational value, and mass newly certified after level $N$ is drawn from the level-$N$ undecided pool. The density of type $\sigma$ is *defined* as that decided limit — never the trivial box count, which is identically $1$.

The Lean statement 
structure CountingModel (q n : ℕ) where
  hq : 2 ≤ q
  decidedCount : FactorizationType → ℕ → ℚ
  undecidedCount : ℕ → ℚ
  decidedCount_nonneg : ∀ σ N, 0 ≤ decidedCount σ N
  undecidedCount_nonneg : ∀ N, 0 ≤ undecidedCount N
  typeMenu : Finset FactorizationType
  typeMenu_degree : ∀ σ ∈ typeMenu, σ.degree = n
  decidedCount_off_menu : ∀ σ N, σ ∉ typeMenu → decidedCount σ N = 0
  box_partition : ∀ N, (∑ σ ∈ typeMenu, decidedCount σ N) + undecidedCount N = (q : ℚ) ^ (n * N)
  decided_mono : ∀ σ, Monotone (fun N => decidedCount σ N / (q : ℚ) ^ (n * N))
  densityVal : FactorizationType → ℚ
  decided_tendsto : ∀ σ, Filter.Tendsto (fun N => decidedCount σ N / (q : ℚ) ^ (n * N))
    Filter.atTop (nhds (densityVal σ))
  upper_tail : ∀ (σ : FactorizationType) (N K : ℕ), N ≤ K →
    decidedCount σ K / (q : ℚ) ^ (n * K)
      ≤ decidedCount σ N / (q : ℚ) ^ (n * N) + undecidedCount N / (q : ℚ) ^ (n * N)

lean/LeanUrat/CountingModel.lean — structure

proved The bracket squeeze: vanishing undecided mass determines the density

The vanishing-undecided hypothesis is the imported termination content (the classifier must eventually decide almost all polynomials): it was discharged unconditionally for the full degree-2 model in 2026-07-22, and its general discharge is the open exhaustion question of the later development work.

In any counting model, both brackets are proved: $\mathrm{decided}(\sigma, N) \le \rho(\sigma) \le \mathrm{decided}(\sigma, N) + \mathrm{undecided}(N)$ for every level $N$. If additionally the undecided mass tends to $0$, then the upper bracket also converges to $\rho(\sigma)$, so the density is the unique value squeezed between brackets of vanishing width.

The Lean statement 
theorem countingDensity_is_squeezed_limit {q n : ℕ} (M : CountingModel q n)
    (hU : UndecidedVanishes M) (σ : FactorizationType) :
    Filter.Tendsto (fun N => M.decidedMeasure σ N + M.undecided N) Filter.atTop
      (nhds (M.countingDensity σ))

lean/LeanUrat/CountingModel.lean — theorem

proved Guard: the box count is the total, never a density

An anti-vacuity record demanded by the project lead: it formally blocks the classic failure mode of "proving" a density theorem that secretly computes the trivial box count 1.

At every precision $N$, the full box measure (identically $1$) equals the sum over all types of the decided measures plus the undecided remainder. The trivial count of all monic degree-$n$ polynomials mod $p^N$ is therefore the partition total across every type — categorically not the density of any single type $\sigma$.

The Lean statement 
theorem box_is_total_not_density (N : ℕ) :
    M.boxMeasure N = (∑ σ ∈ M.typeMenu, M.decidedMeasure σ N) + M.undecided N

lean/LeanUrat/CountingModel.lean — theorem

definition The corrected self-loop collapse: a nested multi-block recursion

Deliberately a predicate that a constructed solution must satisfy, not an axiom asserting a conclusion — the module records the corrected crux after the naive single-geometric-series collapse was found false for s ≥ 3.

The size-$s$ completely-split cluster volume $I_s$ is NOT a single geometric series for $s \ge 3$ (the original blueprint's crux was wrong — the fix is on record). The corrected equation: $I_s \cdot (1 - q^{-w(s)}) = \sum_{P} q^{\underline{r}} \prod_{B \in P} q^{-(|B| + \binom{|B|}{2})} I_{|B|}$, summing over set partitions $P$ of $\{1,\dots,s\}$ into $r \ge 2$ blocks, with $q^{\underline{r}}$ the falling factorial (distinct residue assignments), $w(s) = s(s{+}1)/2 - 1$, and base cases $I_0 = I_1 = 1$. Every block is strictly smaller than $s$ (proved), so the recursion is well-founded.

The Lean statement 
def SatisfiesNested (I : ℕ → ℕ → ℚ) : Prop :=
  (∀ q, I 0 q = 1) ∧ (∀ q, I 1 q = 1) ∧
    ∀ (s q : ℕ), 2 ≤ s → 2 ≤ q →
      I s q * (1 - ((q : ℚ) ^ L5fix.selfLoopExponent s)⁻¹) = nestedRHS I s q

lean/LeanUrat/NestedCollapse.lean — definition

proved The nested recursion reproduces the known closed forms

A machine-checked unit test of the corrected crux against independently derived closed forms (the companion theorem handles $I_2$); the module also upgrades the algebraic pivot identity to the genuine counting limit of finite geometric sums.

Any $I$ satisfying the nested recursion has $I_2 = q/(q+1)$ and $I_3 = q^3(q^2 - q + 1) / \big((q+1)(q^4+q^3+q^2+q+1)\big)$ for all $q \ge 2$: the set partitions of $2$ and $3$ elements into $\ge 2$ blocks are enumerated explicitly (one and four of them), the weights computed, and the pole-free self-loop pivot inverted.

The Lean statement 
theorem nested_reproduces_I3 (I : ℕ → ℕ → ℚ) (hI : SatisfiesNested I) (q : ℕ) (hq : 2 ≤ q) :
    I 3 q = closedI3 q

lean/LeanUrat/NestedCollapse.lean — theorem

definition The axiom census: machine-printed trust footprints for every capstone

Docs cross-check (2026-07-30): the trusted-base table matches the tree — the five measure-route axioms live in PadicMeasure.lean, the GMN index cite at OM/Classifier.lean:1114, the menu-scoped Montes cite at OM/OmLeafFaithful.lean:489, and the only other axiom declarations are deliberately MUTATED refutation probes in OM/ evidence blocks; all censused capstone names resolve, and the scoped root modules carry zero sorries — but the documentation's "exactly one fenced unproved goal" claim (OM/Classifier.lean:1243) is now scoped to this classic corpus only, since the newer Moves*/HC1/HC2/MovesGr development directories carry roughly 40 additional fenced unproved goals outside these footprints.

A compile-time audit file containing 739 axiom-printing directives (one #print axioms line per audited declaration): for each capstone and gate theorem of the project it makes Lean's kernel print the exact set of axioms the proof term uses. This is the ground truth for every footprint claim in the documentation — the mechanism by which "this theorem rests only on Lean's core axioms" or "exactly on this one cited axiom" is machine-verified rather than asserted.

The Lean statement 
#print axioms LeanUrat.Goal.goal_theorem

lean/LeanUrat/AxChk_baseline.lean — definition

The honest boundary

PROVED, from Lean's three built-in logical axioms plus its standard library, with no unproved hypotheses: the complete degree-2 uniformity theorem at every prime (one rational function per quadratic splitting type, undecided remainder vanishing, wild prime 2 included); the order-0 density theorem at general degree; the finiteness of the pattern catalogue with an explicit bound at every degree and the enumerated count of 53 at degree three; the candidate-function solve and its identical checksum; the capstone squeeze as an implication; and the large majority of the roughly 970 public declarations mapped on this page.

PROVED CONDITIONALLY (machine-checked implications from named, displayed hypotheses): the capstone theorem — at any prime whose instance bundle passes the two-clause regularity gate, each density exists and equals its candidate value in three equivalent readings. The hypothesis surface is typed and visible in the statement: a per-prime instance bundle (whose construction from the classifier at every degree beyond the certified cases is the main open work), the regularity gate, and thirteen named placeholder slots reserved for conditions whose vocabulary is not yet built — in the current text these are satisfiable trivially and so impose no constraint yet; the theorem's final strength depends on their eventual content.

CITED (audited literature imports, each with a faithfulness record): the Guàrdia–Montes–Nart theorem of the index, in a form deliberately narrowed after two broader formulations were refuted by explicit polynomials; and the Denef–Igusa cell recursion, used only on an independent measure-theoretic route. Neither enters the two capstones' certificates, which the proof assistant's axiom printout confirms are exactly Lean's core.

VERIFIED COMPUTATIONALLY (general statements are conjectures; every instance in the stated finite ranges checked exactly, cross-checked against an independent computer-algebra factorization routine): the base combinatorial lemmas of the carry analysis — the largest checks cover all 40,378 boundary configurations of one lemma, all 140 cases of a pool census, and a 404,128,692-box partition with zero defects; and the two regularity clauses across their probed ranges.

OPEN (each an explicitly marked unproved statement in the tree, 46 in all at the time of writing): ten named statements in the counting-campaign corpora (six in the branch-tree corpus, four in the value layer), each carrying a written record — two of these are recorded as false at their pinned test tables, a documented blueprint defect awaiting a design decision rather than missing mathematics; two in the tower-kernel corpus; nine construction-phase statements in the existence-bridge corpus; twenty in a superseded graded-ring bridge kept for the record; four in deprecated files retained as history; and one banked bound in the classifier, off the capstone path.

CONJECTURES (stated precisely, nothing certified depends on them being true): exhaustiveness — the classifier decides almost every polynomial at every degree and prime (proved at degree 2, threshold form at general degree); regularity at every prime; and the palindromic functional equation of the candidate functions — proved at tame primes in arXiv:2212.00294 (Poincaré duality), open at wild primes; recorded in this development as an uncertified statement no certified result consumes.
